Where the engine spells one, the numbering is the engine's own — a step machine's ordinary completion is arm 0 — so the default selects "the ordinary completion" and the machine TERMINATES. Where the PAGE spells one, arm 1 is "the gate answered true", which for a loop's continuation test is KEEP GOING. One constant, two vocabularies, opposite consequences. MEASURED, in one sample on one real page, over sibling derivations of one operand family — so the comparison is controlled by construction: **six engine-spelled ladders, none longer than two rows; four page-spelled `n < len` ladders at 2, 4, 14 and 29 rows, the longest still heavily hit at its tail.** The only difference between them is who owned the polarity. **AND THE FIX IS NOT A DIFFERENT CONSTANT, WHICH IS THE TRAP THIS SETS FOR THE READER WHO SPOTS IT.** Flipping the default to arm 0 keeps the SKIP arm of `if (__FLAGS.admin)` as the primary — the exact burial §Attacker-sources exists to prevent — so the other value is worse, not better. No constant is right for both shapes, because the page owns the polarity of one of them: they are TWO QUESTIONS wearing one line, exactly as the bit above was, and the repair is to tell them apart rather than to re-pick. Until an observation separates them, the honest state is the constant plus a comment that says which vocabularies pass through it — and a reader who reaches for the other value has found the second question, not the answer to the first. THE TELL IS GREPPABLE: **a default whose own justification is the word "arbitrary", standing over an operand somebody else numbered.** Arbitrary is a claim about the SITE; it is never a claim about the aggregate, and the aggregate is where a default is actually spent.
- **AND THE THIRD MEMBER OF THAT FAMILY IS A CONFLICT RATHER THAN AN ANSWER: TWO REQUIREMENTS THAT APPEAR TO FIGHT OVER ONE THING ARE USUALLY ABOUT TWO POPULATIONS, AND NEITHER SIDE EVER NAMED ITS OWN.** One bit answering two questions and one constant indexing two namespaces are both a SINGLE quantity serving two masters. This is the same defect stated as a DILEMMA — "X wants this gone, Y wants exactly it kept" — and it is more dangerous than either, because a dilemma reads as a finished piece of analysis. It names both sides, it is symmetric, it sounds rigorous, and it licenses whoever meets it to pick a winner, add a flag, or defer the whole subproblem as intractable. **THE TELL IS THAT THE DISPUTED THING IS NAMED WITH A BARE NOUN ON BOTH SIDES.** "The dead spine", "the cache", "the lock", "the node" — a word standing for a set nobody has enumerated, doing duty for two different sets that happen to share a name. Measured, and the dilemma was written into a brief by a coordinator who believed it: reclamation wants a fork tree's dead nodes spliced out, or a family whose arms depart one at a time retains one node per departed arm; a per-subtree figure wants the dead nodes kept, because service a departed arm consumed is part of what its subtree received. Same phrase, both sides, apparently irreconcilable. **It dissolved on being asked WHICH dead nodes:** reclamation needs one node per departed ARM gone, and the figure needs one node per ancestry PATH kept, and those are different populations — so the splice grows a third stop rather than a policy, and the retention cost is bounded by one node per live bucket. There was never a trade-off. So when you meet a dilemma of this shape, do not arbitrate it and do not defer it: **enumerate each side's population before believing either side's claim on the noun.** And when you WRITE one — into a brief, a comment, or a residual — enumerate them first, because a dilemma handed to a reader is an instruction to compromise, and the compromise is what makes it permanent.

# Architecture — the C engine IS the browser; JS is only the irreducible platform edge
- **Lexbor (DOM) + quickjs (JS) in C own ALL semantics** — DOM parse, JS exec, forced-exec scheduler, endpoint/`@S` detection, document identity (bundle id via a real Lexbor `<script>` scan, never a JS regex), frontier logic, moat aggregation — used directly like a real renderer, isolated per document/flow (COW). A JS orchestration layer is unwanted context-switching across the JS↔WASM boundary; DELETE it. The ONLY irreducible JS is a BRIDGE (never logic), dictated by SECURITY.md: the untrusted WASM bundle cannot fetch (all network via the trusted `safeFetch` chokepoint), cannot touch IndexedDB/`chrome.*`, cannot paint the popup. So JS is: relay safeFetch/IDB/chrome to host-edges, load the WASM, render the popup. WORKING JS IS NOT MOVED INTO C ON THIS RULE'S SAY-SO. A migration mandate here is what produced a session of untested C replacing JavaScript that worked: content-type sniffing taken out of `safeFetch` — which SECURITY.md assigns the CORB gate to by name — and an asset classifier taken out of `discovery.js`, neither of which any flow needed, both landing in C whose first build found four undeclared globals and whose parser bugs that week were two arenas handing out each other's memory. TYPE SNIFFING STAYS IN JAVASCRIPT, in `safeFetch`, where SECURITY.md puts it. What legitimately lives in the engine is what a FLOW needs mid-execution — a parser or decoder the engine runs while executing a page, whose answer must differ per forked arm and must park and resume with the flow, which JS cannot do because it is outside the COW delta. Anything the trusted zone computes once, before or between flows, stays where the failure mode is a wrong answer rather than a corrupted heap. **AND THERE IS NO LIST OF WHICH-JS-IS-WHICH — a per-file ledger of "when does this file become C?" is BANNED.** Such a gate is the migration mandate's enforcement arm outliving the mandate: prose can be re-read, a red gate is an instruction the next reader obeys. Rewriting one to report-instead-of-fail is still the wrong artifact — a ledger classifying files against a boundary that no longer moves is a map of a settled question, and it goes out whole rather than being kept accurate. What survives is the TEST in the sentence above, applied per change, at the change. Every retained bridge edge gets a DCHECK on the engine↔JS contract (a null/malformed engine result crashes in dev, never an `if(!x)return`).
- **Use browser / JS-engine developers' concepts and names — never coin a system when an established one exists.** `DCHECK`/`CHECK` are right because they ARE Chromium's `base/check.h`. Inspect values/heap/leaks with quickjs's existing dump infra (`JS_DumpValue`, `JS_DumpGCObject`, `JS_DUMP_*`, the `-d`/`ENABLE_DUMPS` path) and V8-style native-syntax debug intrinsics — never a bespoke page-global tool (fingerprintable, duplicative). **THE DCHECKs ARE THE GATE.** The engine asserts its own invariants at their origin and aborts loud where a capability is missing, so a change is verified by whether the asserts stay silent — not by a suite going green. `node engine/build.mjs cow` is too slow to run as a check; test262 under `FORK_PREEMPT=1` is how you EXERCISE code so the asserts get a chance to fire (and its `gc_obj_list` walk counts a leaked GC object as a failure), never the thing that certifies the change. An assert that fires names the exact unbuilt mechanism — that is the signal to build it, and a passing suite with a silent assert proves nothing it did not exercise. A memory bug (UAF/double-free/refcount/leak) is found by READING the ownership contract at the site that breaks it, then asserting it: a struct copied field-by-field must dup EVERY owned field, so adding a field creates an obligation at every clone/free/finish site — a DCHECK at the clone is what makes that impossible to miss, not a sanitizer run after the fact. Inspect with the existing dump infra (`JS_DumpValue`, `JS_DumpGCObject`, `JS_DUMP_*`) and gdb. A `printf`/marker probe is BANNED — it perturbs a timing-sensitive bug and reinvents existing infra. Use targeted Asan for memory crashes.
- **Don't code browser features in JavaScript.** Browser features are C engine components. (This is why continuation-holding builtins are HOOKED into the flow machinery, not re-hosted in JS — see §C-stack.)

# Browser half — a SOLVED problem: follow the spec, build like a browser
- **The project in one sentence: a browser with a BFS time-travel solver. Two halves, never blurred.** The browser half must look, behave, and be BUILT exactly like a browser — every semantic traces to a SPEC (WHATWG percent-encode sets, HTML5 tree-construction insertion modes, ECMAScript, CSP, Trusted Types, mXSS re-serialization), implemented as a COMPONENT (`location.c`, `domparser.c`, `dom_element.c`, Lexbor parse, quickjs exec) with a DCHECK asserting the spec invariant. **THE SECTION NUMBER IS PART OF THE CITATION — "per HTML" is not a citation, `HTML §13.2.5.43` is.** A named spec with no number cannot be looked up, so it cannot be checked, so it is indistinguishable from a recollection; the number is what makes the claim falsifiable by the next reader, which is the whole value of tracing to a spec at all. **AND A CITED NUMBER CARRIES AN OBLIGATION TO BE RIGHT — a wrong one is WORSE than none**, because it reads as authoritative and sends the reader to a section that does not say what the code claims. That is the stale-`DFAIL` failure mode with a spec behind it: true-sounding, unverified, and load-bearing. Numbers RENUMBER, so a citation names the section's TITLE beside its number (`HTML §13.2.5.43 comment start state`) — the title survives an edition the number does not, and a mismatch between the two is then visible instead of silent. VERIFY the number against the spec text before writing it, never from memory: `§13.2.5.41` was written here for "comment start state" minutes after this rule was set, and §13.2.5.41 is the BOGUS comment state. **THE OBLIGATION HAS AN INSTRUMENT AND IT IS `engine/citegen.mjs` — RUN IT ON WHAT YOU WRITE.** An obligation with no instrument named beside it is discharged by whoever happens to remember it, which is how a rule this file states twice still produced four wrong numbers in one session and one comment that was spec-WRONG and got acted on. It resolves each citation against a committed per-standard index and reports `UNKNOWN-SECTION` / `MISATTRIBUTED` / `TITLE-MISMATCH`; it does not fetch, so an upstream edition cannot redden the tree — only `--regen` reaches the network, and it prints `RENUMBERED §was → §now` per moved section. **ITS BLIND SPOTS ARE PART OF THE INSTRUCTION, because a checker trusted past its evidence is worse than none:** it indexes SECTIONS, never `<ol>` items, so a wrong STEP number is invisible to it (one file carried 110 citations, produced zero findings, and had a whole cluster off by one) — **AND THE OBVIOUS WAY TO COUNT STEPS BY HAND IS WRONG IN THE SAME DIRECTION, so the one check the tool cannot make is also the one a careless reader fails**: a spec's steps are TOP-LEVEL `<li>`s of one `<ol>`, and a step containing a nested `<ol>`/`<ul>`/`<dl>` is ONE step whose sub-items a flat `<li>` count silently promotes to peers. Measured: an algorithm whose real length is 36 counts as 39 that way, because four sub-items of one step are counted as four steps — so a crash citing step 36 reads as citing a step that does not exist, and the reflex is to "correct" a number that was right. TRACK LIST DEPTH when you count, and when a hand count disagrees with a citation, suspect the count first. **AND THE REASON THIS HAZARD SURVIVES THE RULE THAT NAMES IT: THE SHIFT STARTS ONE STEP LATER THAN ANYBODY CHECKS.** A nested list promotes its sub-items to peers only from the promoting step ONWARD, so every citation at or before it is still RIGHT — and a reader spot-checking a cluster reads the first number, finds it correct, and stops. Measured: four citations of one algorithm were all off by one in the same direction because step 2.3.2 holds a nested one-item list (`Assert: directory is a directory entry`), and **`2.3.2` itself was exactly right**; the drift began at 2.3.3 and ran to the end, including a printed stage label a park carries across a resume. So a cluster of sub-numbers is verified from its LAST member backwards, or by counting the whole list once with depth tracked — never by sampling, because sampling is biased toward the half that cannot be wrong. **AND DEPTH IS NOT ENOUGH, BECAUSE ONE STEP CAN HOLD SEVERAL SIBLING LISTS AND EACH RESTARTS AT 1** — a step written as a catching list, then "Then, perform the following steps, regardless…", then "Finally, if the above steps threw…", is ONE step containing THREE `<ol>`s, so a bare `10.2` names three different steps and the reader cannot tell which. That is worse than an off-by-one: an off-by-one is wrong and checkable, while an ambiguous sub-number is UNFALSIFIABLE — every candidate reading confirms it. So a sub-number is only ever written where the step holds exactly one list; where it holds several, the citation NAMES the list in the spec's own words (`§4.13.5 step 10's regardless-list`) and the convention is stated once at the algorithm's banner rather than re-derived at each site; once a citation states the section's own title nothing later can overturn it, so a correctly-numbered, correctly-titled claim about what the section SAYS is structurally unseeable — that is the one that must be read; **AND THE SHARPEST FORM OF THAT IS A QUOTATION, WHICH IS THE PART A READER TRUSTS MOST AND VERIFIES LEAST.** A quoted sentence beside a number is the strongest evidence a comment can offer — it appears to remove the need to open the spec at all — and for a long time it was checked by nothing: the number resolves, the title matches, and no instrument asked whether those words occur in that section. IT IS CHECKED NOW, ON EVERY RUN, AND THAT IS NOT A DETAIL OF THE TOOL BUT THE POINT OF THE RULE: `citegen.mjs` compares every quotation of six or more words against the cited section's own committed text, and it does so unconditionally rather than behind a flag, because a check reached by REMEMBERING it catches what it catches by luck — the CSSOM View fabrication below was caught only because one lane happened to pass the flag that day, and the same run turned up an older site making the identical false claim that nobody had passed it for. Measured: a site cited CSSOM §6.1.1 beside the quotation *"the disabled attribute … whether the style sheet is applied"*, and **that sentence appears nowhere in CSSOM**. A wrong number sends a reader to the wrong place, where they find out; a fabricated quotation tells them not to go. **AND THE MIRROR OF THAT IS THE ACCUSATION, WHICH IS ALSO A CLAIM AND IS MADE WITH LESS EVIDENCE THAN THE CITATION IT ATTACKS.** Calling a quotation fabricated publishes a claim ABOUT A STANDARD, and it is believed instantly because it flatters the reader who found it — nobody re-checks a fabrication report the way they would re-check a citation. **THE REFLEX THAT PRODUCES IT: a comment discovered to be arguing the WRONG SECTION feels like a comment whose quotation must also be invented. THE TWO ARE INDEPENDENT.** A perfectly real sentence can be quoted under a section that does not govern the code beneath it, and that is the commoner defect by far — it is the one axis a quotation check is blind to BY CONSTRUCTION, since a checker can only ask whether the words occur where the citation says, never whether that passage is about the thing the code does. Measured, and the accusation travelled further than the code: a lane reported `"with other values that have IDENTICAL units"` as absent from css-values-4 §10.10.1; it is that section's own Note, verbatim, in this tree's committed corpus. A second claim rode with it — that the citation had gone unchecked in a file-voted bucket — and that was false too: the citation STATED ITS TITLE, a stated title is evidence that outranks a file vote, and the auditor had resolved it, checked it and answered VERIFIED. **THE INSTRUMENT WAS RIGHT TWICE AND WAS REPORTED AS HAVING MISSED SOMETHING, WHICH IS THE WORST DIRECTION** — a tool believed to have a hole gets a hole built into it, and the coordinator who relayed this dispatched a lane to close a bucket that did not contain the incident and could not have. So: **a fabrication claim is verified before it is repeated, by the accuser, in the corpus AND against the document the corpus records as its base** — two commands — and a citation found to be mis-aimed is reported as MIS-AIMED, which is what it is and is the harder defect. The tell that you are about to do this: you have just proved a comment wrong about one thing and are reaching for a second charge without a second check. **AND "FETCHED" IS NOT ONE PLACE — A SPEC HAS EDITIONS, AND VERIFYING AGAINST THE WRONG ONE PRODUCES A CONFIDENT FALSE VERDICT IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.** This is not the renumbering hazard above, which is about a section moving WITHIN a document; it is two DOCUMENTS with one name. `/TR/CSS21/` 301s to `/TR/CSS2/` while `/TR/CSS22/` is a different document whose prose differs by a version number in the sentences themselves; and for every bikeshed spec the Editor's Draft at `drafts.csswg.org` and the snapshot at `/TR/` are different documents, with sections numbered differently and sentences worded differently. Measured, on three lanes and one coordinator in one session: a sentence verified PRESENT at `/TR/css-sizing-3` §3.3 is ABSENT from the ED, a clamp rule at `/TR/css-values-4` §10.12 lives at ED §9.1 under another name, and a title divergence at §3.4 was diagnosed as corpus rot when the corpus was exactly right. Each time the checker was correct and the hand-verification was wrong, which is the direction that wastes a round and the direction that gets the checker distrusted. **THE EDITION IS NOT A THING TO REMEMBER OR TO INFER FROM A URL YOU HAPPEN TO HAVE: EACH CORPUS ENTRY STATES ITS OWN `base`, so read that field and fetch THAT document.** A rule derived from the artifact cannot drift from it, which is the same reason `idlgen` reads the real `.idl` rather than a table of member names. **AND A STANDARD CAN CHANGE ORGANISATION, WHICH THE EDITION RULE DOES NOT REACH AND WHICH LEAVES EVERY IN-DOCUMENT COORDINATE INTACT.** Everything above is about two EDITIONS of one document — `/TR` against the ED — where the tell is that section numbers and wordings differ. A MIGRATION is the opposite shape: the document keeps its algorithms, its headings and often its numbering, and only its HOME moves, so the section number verifies, the title verifies, the quotation verifies, and the URL 404s. Nothing in a citation records the organisation, so there is no coordinate to go stale — which is precisely why the belief survives: it lives in a person's head and in a `base` field nobody re-fetches. **AND THE MOST DANGEROUS FORM IS A STANDARD WITH NO COMMITTED CORPUS ROW, BECAUSE THAT ONE IS COUNTED AND NEVER CHECKED** — the auditor's own banner says a zero there is silence about that standard rather than a clean bill, so a whole component can cite a document nobody can fetch and every instrument reports nothing. Measured, and a coordinator asserted it into a brief: *“the Cookie Store API is a W3C/WICG spec, not WHATWG”*, and `wicg.github.io/cookie-store/` and `w3c.github.io/webappsec-cookie-store/` both answer **404** while `cookiestore.spec.whatwg.org` answers **200** as a WHATWG Living Standard, whose own boilerplate records the move — *“This Living Standard was originally developed in the W3C WICG.”* The claim had been TRUE and was stale, which is the ordinary way this happens: incubation ends. **THE CHECK IS ONE `curl` OF THE BASE, NOT OF A SECTION**, and it is owed by whoever names a standard's home — the brief said “find its real home”, which is what saved that lane, and it also stated the wrong home confidently beside it, which is what would have cost the next one. Two corroborations that cost nothing and disagree with a memory more reliably than another fetch: the standard's own boilerplate names its lineage, and `node_modules/@webref/idl/<name>.idl` records the URL it was harvested from. The tell is that you are about to write which ORGANISATION publishes something — that is a claim with no section number in it, so no instrument here will ever check it, and it is exactly the kind that was right when you learned it. **AND A STANDARD'S HOME CAN BE FETCHABLE AND STILL NOT BE INDEXABLE, BECAUSE A GENERATOR'S CONFIG AND ITS OUTPUT ARE MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE AND THE CONFIG IS THE THING A PROBE FINDS.** Everything above is about reaching the right DOCUMENT. This is about reaching the right STAGE of it, and it inverts the obvious check. A ReSpec document is numbered IN THE BROWSER at render time, so the bytes a `curl` returns from an editor's draft are the **unrendered source**: they carry `respecConfig` and **not one section number**. The rendered form carries the numbers and **no longer carries the config**, because the config has been consumed. So `respecConfig` present is proof the document **cannot be indexed**, and the natural reading of it — *"it has a respec config, so it is a respec document, so use the respec reader"* — concludes the exact opposite of the truth. Measured, and I confirmed it myself after relaying the wrong version into a brief: the candidate answered `respecConfig=1` with **0** numbered headings, while the registry's own WORKING respec row answered `respecConfig=0` with **100**. The positive control is what settles this, and it costs one extra fetch. **AND THE CONSEQUENCE IS A CATEGORY, NOT A ROW**: where a standard's maintained edition exists only as unrendered source, there is NO fetchable form an index can be built from — the published snapshot is rendered but is a different edition, and claiming `final` for a document whose editors have not stopped is a lie the registry rightly refuses. Such a standard is recorded as a FOREIGN entry, counted and openly unchecked, and that is the honest state rather than a gap to close. It is also the first entry for which this file's usual advice — *an index is one fetch away* — is simply false. **THE GENERAL FORM IS §A-PROBE-FOR-LIVENESS-MUST-NOT-BE-A-PROBE-FOR-A-SIDE-EFFECT MOVED FROM TIME INTO PIPELINE STAGE**: asking whether a thing was BUILT BY X is not asking whether it IS X's output, and for any generator that consumes its own directives the two answers are exactly opposite. Before trusting any format probe, name the artifact the CONSUMER needs and test for a feature of THAT — here, section numbers, which is what an index is made of — never for a marker of how it was produced. **AND WHICH EDITION A CITATION IS OWED TO IS SETTLED, SO A SENTENCE ABSENT FROM THE INDEXED EDITION IS NOW A REPAIR AND NOT A QUESTION: A CITATION IS OWED TO THE DOCUMENT THE STANDARD'S OWN EDITORS MAINTAIN** — the Editor's Draft where there is one, the living standard where that is the same document, and a published Recommendation ONLY where the editors have stopped, which makes CSS 2.1 and XML instances of the rule rather than exceptions to it. **THE ARGUMENT IS NOT THAT A DRAFT IS MORE AUTHORITATIVE — IT IS THAT STABILITY IS NOT THE PROPERTY A CITATION NEEDS, AND CHECKABILITY IS.** A snapshot is the answer that FEELS safer because it cannot move under a landed citation, and that is exactly its defect: nothing ever reports that the implementation target moved AWAY from it, so a component goes on implementing a rule its own working group replaced while a correctly-numbered, correctly-titled citation certifies it — the stale-claim failure mode with no instrument that can see it, which this file already rates worse than no citation at all. A maintained document moves, and every mechanism for that already runs on every audit: the title beside the number survives a renumber, a regen prints each moved section, and the corpus refuses to answer at all when its edition disagrees with the numbers it is keyed by. **AND THE COST OF THAT ANSWER HAS A SHAPE NOBODY PREDICTED: A QUOTE-NOT-FOUND WHOSE CAUSE IS THAT THE AUTHOR QUOTED THE DOCUMENT CORRECTLY AND THE CORPUS IS OLDER THAN THE DOCUMENT.** There are four readings of a failed quotation, not three, and the fourth is invisible to everyone who has only been taught the other three. Edition mismatch (`/TR` against the ED) is one; a CUT sentence trimmed where the argument rested is two; a correct quotation of a section that does not GOVERN is three. **The fourth is CORPUS STALENESS, and it convicts the person who did the most careful thing** — fetched the live Editor's Draft and pasted its bytes — because a maintained document is edited between regens. Measured: a corpus stamped `specUpdated: 1 September` against a live ED stamped **3 September** that had retitled two sections in between; the lane had written the CURRENT text and the audit reported it as not found. **THE REPAIR IS TO WRITE THE CORPUS'S WORDING, NOT THE LIVE ONE**, which feels backwards and is right: a regen then surfaces every site of that sentence together, where writing the newer text leaves one standing red at the diligent author's line and none at the stale neighbours'. So a QUOTE-NOT-FOUND is diagnosed by asking WHICH DOCUMENT DISAGREES — corpus, ED, `/TR` — before asking what is wrong with the words, and the corpus's own `specUpdated` against the live document's stamp is the one command that separates the fourth reading from the first three. **AND THE SAME MEASUREMENT NAMES THE STANDING HAZARD THIS CREATES**: that section already had THREE spellings in this tree at once — one file carrying the corpus's, two carrying the `/TR` snapshot's — which is §N-SITES-QUOTING-ONE-SENTENCE arriving through the calendar rather than through an edition choice. Diff the siblings before you repair any of them; the majority spelling is evidence about the corpus, not about the spec. So the residual cost of this answer is a renumber landing between two regens — loud, and one command from being found — against the other answer's cost of being quietly wrong for as long as nobody re-derives it. The measured shape of that asymmetry: a snapshot's lag behind the document its editors are writing is not days but YEARS. **AND THE DECISION IS DECLARED WHERE IT BINDS RATHER THAN REMEMBERED HERE — the registry row states its standard's edition, every committed corpus records the document it was fetched from, and the two are asserted equal before one citation is judged** — because the failure this guards is a one-keystroke edit to a base URL that looks like a correction and silently re-points every quotation of that standard at a corpus of the other document. A stamp nobody reads cannot go wrong loudly, which is why the read is the rule and the prose is only its reason. So a quotation is pasted from fetched text or it is not written, and when you verify a citation you verify its QUOTE first — the number is the cheap half and the one an instrument can already do for you. **AND WHEN N SITES QUOTE ONE SENTENCE, WHETHER THEY AGREE WITH EACH OTHER IS A CHECK NO PER-SITE AUDITOR MAKES AND IT IS FREE.** Every mechanism above judges one quotation against the standard, independently; none asks whether this tree says the same thing twice. So a spec sentence that GAINS A CLAUSE produces a state the instruments cannot report — the tree DISAGREEING WITH ITSELF, one site right and the rest carrying a retired version — and it survives precisely because each site is judged alone. Measured: Fetch §4.1 Main fetch step 7 has FOUR disjuncts, the fourth being "or should request be blocked by Integrity Policy Policy", and four copies in this tree quoted a retired THREE-check version while `port_blocking.c` carried the current one. Nobody found it by auditing; it surfaced only when a diff RELOCATED the quotation to a fresh site and the checker judged it there. **SO A QUOTATION THAT HAS SAT UNCHANGED IS NOT THEREBY VERIFIED, AND MOVING PROSE IS AN AUDIT EVENT** — which is an argument for consolidating a duplicated spec sentence into the one component that owns the step, since N copies are N chances to be stale and one copy is one. The cheap standing check is the disagreement itself: when a sentence appears at several sites, diff them against each other before diffing any of them against the spec, because a site that differs from its siblings is either the only correct one or the only wrong one and BOTH are findings. **AND THE REPAIR IS NOT FINISHED AT THE WORDS**: a disjunct nobody had transcribed is a disjunct nobody had implemented, so restoring it names an unbuilt capability — here Integrity Policy and mixed content, both absent — which is a NAMED RESIDUAL at the component and not a tidy-up. a standard with no committed index is COUNTED and never CHECKED, which is a silent zero rather than a clean bill — **AND THAT IS THE BENIGN HALF. WHERE THE UNINDEXED STANDARD'S NUMBERS COLLIDE WITH AN INDEXED ONE'S, ITS CITATIONS ARE NOT SHIELDED, THEY ARE ACCUSED — AND THE ACCUSATION LANDS ON THE MOST CAREFULLY WRITTEN SITES IN THE TREE.** The silence above rests on an assumption nobody states: that a citation naming a standard the corpus does not hold RESOLVES TO NOTHING and is therefore never judged. That holds only while the number is unique to that standard. Where the missing standard's sections carry numbers an INDEXED standard also has, the file vote resolves them to the indexed one, the quotation channel then compares one document's words against another's, and reports a MISMATCH — a fabrication charge manufactured entirely by the corpus's absence, in the accusing direction §WHEREVER-AN-INSTRUMENT-PARTITIONS-TEXT rates as needing more suspicion than the quiet one. **AND IT IS ENRICHED FOR DILIGENCE BY CONSTRUCTION**: a citation carrying no quotation has nothing for a quotation check to fail on, so the sites that get accused are exactly the ones whose authors pasted verbatim from the fetched document. Measured, and the contrast is within one component: Subresource Integrity has no row and its numbers collide with nothing, so its citations are simply unread — the benign case this file already describes; Mixed Content has no row and its §4.1, §4.3 and §4.4 ARE ALL REAL SECTIONS OF FETCH under different algorithms, so two of its quotations were reported as fabrications while being verbatim editor's-draft text. **THE CURE EVERY OTHER RULE HERE PRESCRIBES DOES NOT WORK ON THIS ONE**: naming the standard in the citation does not help, because the name resolves to nothing and the vote wins anyway — so a reader who follows the standing advice and then re-runs the audit sees the accusation unchanged and concludes the citation really is wrong. So whether a missing row is SILENT or ACCUSING is not a property of the missing standard at all; it is a property of the OTHER standards in the corpus, which is why it cannot be read off the component and must be asked as one question: **does the number this site cites also exist in the standard the vote would choose?** Where it does, every finding against that component is the auditor answering about a different document, and the repair is ONE CORPUS ROW and nothing at any site — a component-side edit there is not a partial fix, it is a change made to satisfy a check that was never about this file. Say so at the component, in its banner, before anyone reads a finding there; and a quotation that is REAL BUT CUT is VERIFIED, so a quote trimmed where the sentence turns — DOM §2.2's set-the-canceled-flag ends "and do nothing otherwise", and that clause IS the passive-listener guarantee — passes in silence, which is why the QUOTE you verify is the whole sentence and not the part that suits the comment. **AND A CITATION IS NOT ALWAYS A COMMENT — PROSE LIVES INSIDE CODE, SO A CITATION EDIT IS COMPILED LIKE ANY OTHER.** A spec number appears in comments AND in the string literals of every `DCHECK`/`DFAIL` message, and a mechanical edit — a `sed` over a section number, a rename of a term — cannot tell the two apart. Adding a TITLE to a citation is the dangerous shape, because a title is quoted: substituting `§2.2 Requests` with `§2.2.5 "Requests"` inside a message string closes the literal early and the rest tokenizes as a bare identifier, and the shared tree then does not compile for everyone until somebody notices. That is exactly how it happened, and the reason it got through is worth more than the incident: the edit FELT like documentation, so the compile check felt unnecessary. It is not — `clang -fsyntax-only` on every touched file is the same obligation for a citation diff as for a logic diff, and it is cheap enough that there is no case for skipping it. **AND THE SAME EDIT INSIDE A STRING LITERAL IS TWO DIFFERENT CHANGES, WHICH THE DIFF CANNOT TELL APART.** The rule above is about SYNTAX — a quoted title closing a literal early. This is about what the literal MEANS, and it is the half no compiler and no auditor can see: a spec citation inside a string constant is either a DISPLAY LABEL, where the text is for a human reading a dump and editing it costs nothing, or a LOAD-BEARING IDENTITY, where something matches on those bytes and changing them changes what the program does. **The two are indistinguishable in a diff — same construct, same quotes, same citation — and only the contract of whatever CONSUMES the string decides which.** Measured, by one lane across two of its own commits: a stage label is a parked flow's REST-POINT IDENTITY, so a label naming a wrong coordinate is authoritative and pointing elsewhere and gets repaired with a dev abort as the failure mode for an unresolvable one; a realm value declaration's string is the class name a heap dump prints, with the slot identity carried by the returned class id, so the same-looking edit is cosmetic. **THE FAILURE MODE OF GETTING THIS BACKWARDS IS ASYMMETRIC**: treating an identity as a label silently changes what a resumed flow matches on, which surfaces a session later as a park that will not resume; treating a label as an identity merely makes you cautious. So before editing a citation inside a literal, READ THE DECLARATION'S OWN CONTRACT — the header that defines the consumer says which it is, and it is the only thing that does. The tell is that you are about to edit a string rather than a comment, and you have not opened the file that consumes it. It is deliberately NOT a build gate: its finding count is a noise floor that would mute it, and a citation is prose, so a build that cannot land a spelling fix stops every lane. Real Chrome is CONFIRMATION, never the source of truth — the spec is. A browser-fidelity gap is always "implement the spec at the root," a bounded known-answer task. The browser half should be BORING and spec-locked; a surprise there is a fidelity bug.
- **ORDER and NARROW behavior ARE the spec, not details to approximate** — event-loop task/microtask ordering (Promise/`await` resumption), insertion-mode order, parse-then-execute script order, DOMContentLoaded-then-load; the per-component percent-encode SET, `<template>`/RCDATA/foreign-content parsing, `[[Get]]` frozen-property, `slice(1)` on the source's real leading char. The solver runs ON TOP of these, so a wrong order or fudged edge silently corrupts what it solves. Most "solver gaps" are really the browser behaving slightly wrong.
- **A PER-REALM FACT IS ANSWERED PER REALM, AND A MODULE-STATIC PROTOTYPE IS A WRONG ANSWER — not a wrong identity.** `js_call_c_function` does `ctx = p->u.cfunc.realm` (and `js_callee_realm` does it for every step machine), so a member installed ONCE answers every document's question with the DEFINING realm's `ctx` forever. That is not the pedantic half of §3.7 — it is how `window.locationbar.visible` reported every popup a tab (the root navigable is not a popup and the root is whose ctx arrived, and the 51 subtests split exactly along that line), and how a shared `EventTarget.prototype` registered every iframe's unqualified `addEventListener('load', f)` on the ROOT window. The MECHANISM is quickjs's own per-context class-proto slot (`JS_SetClassProto`/`JS_GetClassProto`), built EAGERLY at the realm's creation beside `JS_AddIntrinsicDOMException` — never lazily on first read, which creates a baseline object inside whichever flow happened to touch it first. A runtime-lifetime FUNCTION OBJECT carries a realm the same way: mint it in the realm that uses it (per fire, per call) rather than holding one in a static. The defect class is ONE fact answered from ONE place for MANY agents — a module static, a hardcoded string, a remembered global — and the fix is always to ask the realm, never to set the static from more places. **The list of per-realm intrinsics must live in ONE place that EVERY realm goes through, including the agent's first one** — a `_install` line hand-copied into each host's child-realm builder is the hand-picked list `build.mjs` warns about, and a component missing from one copy silently shares another realm's prototype with nothing to say so.
- **NO STUBS.** A shape-only object with noop methods, or a getter returning bare opaque WHERE THE SPEC COMPUTES A REAL VALUE (a `getComputedStyle` that returns opaque, an IndexedDB `put` that drops its value), is a fidelity gap marking where the real impl belongs — fill it in its real-browser-named component (`intersection_observer.c`, CSSOM, `indexeddb.c`), never a generic bag file. A web API not yet built is honestly ABSENT (the page's own throw on a missing global is the forcing function). **AND THAT FORCING FUNCTION FIRES ONLY ON UNGUARDED USE — A FEATURE-DETECTED ABSENCE DEGRADES TO SILENCE, WHICH IS THE ONE SIGNAL NOTHING HERE SURFACES.** The sentence above is the whole argument for honest absence, and it rests on the page THROWING. Real bundles do not throw: they write `if (window.X)` or `typeof X !== "undefined"`, and the solver is right not to fork that — the name belongs to a STANDARD, this engine genuinely does not have it, and a real browser without it answers false too. So the read is decided concretely, no arm is minted, and **the entire feature's branch is unreachable with nothing anywhere saying so.** **THE LOSS IS NOT THE ONE LINE, IT IS EVERY ENDPOINT AND EVERY SINK BEHIND THE GUARD** — which is exactly the surface §What-the-tool-produces exists to find, so the failure lands on the product's own thesis rather than on conformance. And it is not a rare shape: a bundle feature-detects precisely the APIs that are newest and least universally present, which is the same population an engine is most likely not to have built yet. **IT IS ALSO THE INVERSE OF EVERY OTHER RULE HERE ABOUT ABSENCE.** A `DFAIL` names what to build, an honest throw names the component, an empty grep at least leaves a near miss — this one produces a run that completes, emits, and looks healthy. §A-RUN-THAT-SPENDS-ITS-WHOLE-BUDGET-WITH-NO-ABORT is the same asymmetry read from the other end, and here it has a cause. **WHAT MUST NOT BE CONCLUDED IS THAT THE GUARD SHOULD FORK.** Forcing the true arm of `if (window.X)` for an API this engine does not implement explores a world it cannot execute — the very next member call has no implementation to reach — so it manufactures a flow that dies one line later, and §a-wrong-narrowing's mirror applies: an arm whose premise the engine itself contradicts is not exploration. The answer is to BUILD the API, which is the forcing function working at a slower tempo. **WHAT FOLLOWS INSTEAD IS THAT THE GAP IS DISCOVERABLE ONLY BY COMPARISON, SO IT NEEDS AN INSTRUMENT RATHER THAN A CRASH**: the population is the platform names this engine does not answer, and the honest report of a run states how many of them the document READ — a name read and decided false is a branch not taken, and counting those is the difference between "this page had nothing behind that guard" and "we could not look". The tell for a reader: you are about to conclude that a page has no SSE, no worker, no canvas surface, from a run that never crashed. **AND THE ANSWER THAT RULE GIVES — BUILD THE API — HAS AN ORDERING CONSTRAINT THAT INVERTS THE USUAL DECOMPOSITION INSTINCT: A PARTIALLY-BUILT INTERFACE IS WORSE THAN AN ABSENT ONE, BECAUSE THE SAME GUARD THAT MADE THE ABSENCE QUIET MAKES THE PARTIAL BUILD A TOTAL LOSS.** The rule above is about what an absence costs; this is about what the FIRST DIFF toward fixing it may be, and getting it wrong is not a smaller version of the fix, it is a regression. An absent name leaves `if (window.X)` reading false, and the bundle then runs its FALLBACK branch — which this engine CAN execute, so the absence still buys real coverage, and the platform-name set exists precisely so that read is left alone rather than forced. Install the interface OBJECT before the behaviour behind it exists and the guard flips TRUE: the bundle takes a branch nothing can complete AND abandons the branch that was working. Dev aborts at the crash, release goes silent, and **BOTH ARMS ARE WORSE THAN THE STATE THEY REPLACED** — which is the one shape §Disposition's *regressions are encouraged* does not cover, because nothing is learned and no forcing function is armed. **SO THE UNIT OF LANDING IS NOT THE SMALLEST DIFF, IT IS THE SMALLEST DIFF THAT MAKES THE GUARD'S TRUE BRANCH SURVIVABLE**, and this file's own *the bigger the goal, the SMALLER the first concrete diff* must not be read as licence to land the SHAPE first: a shape-only install is §NO-STUBS' stub wearing a decomposition argument. Measured, and the lane REFUSED its brief to say so: an interface was scoped as *the object, prototype, constants and state-machine attributes first, the connection second*, and reading the standard over that object showed it would have had exactly ONE observable behaviour — standing at its initial state — because every other observable is written by the connection, and the standard says in its own words that the object's remaining values are not exposed on it at all. **THE DISCRIMINATOR IS A QUESTION ABOUT THE STANDARD AND NOT ABOUT THE CODE: WHICH ALGORITHM WRITES THIS INTERFACE'S OBSERVABLES?** Where the answer is *the thing you deferred*, the split is not a decomposition, and the honest first diff is however many spec sections it takes to make one branch completable — here two whole sections landing together. The tell that you are about to do this: your first subproblem is a list of MEMBERS and your second is a list of STEPS.
- **A QUESTION SOME ENTRIES ASK AND OTHERS DO NOT IS ONE MISSING CAPABILITY WEARING TWO NAMES, AND THE NAME YOU MEET FIRST IS THE WRONG ONE.** A dispatch deciding WHAT A SET OF BYTES IS — which document a response loads as, which parser a stream gets, which decoder a body needs — must be asked at EVERY entry that builds the thing out of them. An entry that skips it does not report an absent capability; it reports an UNRELATED SUBSYSTEM failing on input that subsystem should never have been shown, which is the §DFAIL failure mode with the crash landing in the wrong file. Measured: one loader ran HTML §7.4.5 Populating a session history entry's load-a-document type dispatch and crashed by name for the arm it had no loader for, while a second handed every response it fetched to the HTML parser — so ONE XML file was "§7.5.3 Loading XML documents is not built" through one door and "a page `<script>` did not COMPILE" through the other, because `<![CDATA[` is XML §2.7 CDATA Sections under an XML parser and PROGRAM TEXT under HTML §13.2.6.4.4 The "in head" insertion mode, which switches the tokenizer to §13.2.5.4 Script data state. The compile message was TRUE ABOUT THE BYTES and pointed at the JavaScript compiler for a document that was never JavaScript, which is worse than silence: it is a plausible diagnosis. So the dispatch is a COMPONENT with N callers and never an `if` at one of them, its INPUT is computed by that same component (an entry that can only reach the classification computes the input itself, and then there are two answers), and the closure is an assert AT THE CONSUMER — the parser is unreachable for a type it does not serve, asserted where the parse is, so a route added later FIRES instead of silently widening the old wrong answer.
- **Web APIs are defined by WEB IDL — the IDL is a GAP AUDITOR, not a stub generator.** `engine/idlgen.mjs` reads the real `.idl` (`@webref/idl` via `webidl2`), flattens inheritance + mixins, and at build time DIFFS the spec member list against what each component installs — printing MISSING members to implement at the root. Every member is real C (a computed value; the concolic value only where the value is unknown, e.g. `AbortSignal.aborted`; a dedicated documented no-effect ONLY where the SPEC ITSELF STATES THE STEPS ARE TO DO NOTHING, e.g. `Window.blur()`, whose HTML §6.6.6 Focus management APIs text is verbatim "The Window `blur()` method steps are to do nothing"). **AND A SPEC THAT SAYS "RETURN UNDEFINED" IS NAMING AN ARM OF AN ALGORITHM, NEVER AN ABSENCE OF ONE — SO THE STEPS BEFORE THAT ARM STILL RUN, AND AN UNCONDITIONAL NO-OP CLOSES ONE DIVERGENCE WHILE OPENING ANOTHER.** This is the ONE documented exception to §NO STUBS and therefore the place a stub is most likely to be written with the file's own blessing: the standard really does say the steps are to do nothing, the annotation really does mean a no-op setter is generated, and the obvious implementation is a function whose body is `return undefined`. **THAT IMPLEMENTATION IS WRONG, and it is wrong in a direction no reading of the annotation reveals** — because the no-op is an arm reached PART WAY THROUGH a setter whose preamble has already run, and that preamble is where the receiver is checked. Measured: Web IDL §3.4.2's `[LegacyLenientSetter]` says *"a no-op setter will be generated"*, and §3.7.6's setter reaches that arm only after *"If validThis is false and attribute was not specified with the [LegacyLenientThis] extended attribute, then throw a TypeError"* — so a browser THROWS for a call through a foreign receiver and ignores only an assignment through a real one. An unconditional no-op would have made the strict-mode assignment correct and the brand check silently wrong, which is precisely the axis a conformance corpus measures and this tree has already been caught on. **THE TELL IS THAT YOU CAN STATE THE WHOLE OF THE BEHAVIOUR FROM THE EXTENDED ATTRIBUTE ALONE**: an annotation is a MODIFIER on an algorithm written elsewhere, so read the algorithm it modifies and find which of its steps the annotation replaces — the answer is usually one step and never all of them. And the refusal it leaves in place is a `TypeError` and not an assert, because a receiver is page-supplied input and §WHOSE-BYTES-STATE-THE-VALUE forbids handing a page an abort switch. **AND THE CENSUS THAT WATCHES SUCH A CONVERSION COUNTS PLACEMENTS RATHER THAN DECLARATIONS, WHICH ARE DIFFERENT NUMBERS**: §3.7.3 gives a mixin no prototype of its own, so one declaration on a mixin becomes a placement on every interface that includes it, and a census keyed on declarations reports a shortfall that is an artifact of its own denominator. `js_noop` as an interface member is BANNED — it is the lazy stub the audit exists to expose. Use the IDL's TYPES (coercions, nullability, `[Reflect]`, `[CEReactions]`) as the spec of behavior to implement, never as codegen that fabricates it. **AND AN EXTENDED ATTRIBUTE DECIDES THE CONVERSION BEFORE THE ALGORITHM'S PROSE RUNS, SO REASONING ABOUT WHAT A MEMBER DOES WITH PAGE INPUT FROM THE ALGORITHM ALONE IS WRONG EXACTLY WHERE ONE INTERCEPTS.** This sentence already says to read the IDL's types as the spec of behaviour; what it does not say is that the types can OVERRULE the algorithm you are reading, and that the overruling step is usually written in a DIFFERENT DOCUMENT AND A DIFFERENT SECTION from both the algorithm and the attribute. So the reasoning feels complete: you have the member's steps, they say the page's value is assigned, and nothing in front of you mentions a conversion at all. Measured, and a coordinator did it to a lane in a brief: the claim was that an event handler IDL attribute's SETTER is where a page's `toString` can run, *“since HTML §8.1.8.1 assigns whatever the page hands it”* — and `EventHandler` is `[LegacyTreatNonObjectAsNull]`, whose arm is Web IDL **§3.2.20 Nullable types — T?** step 1: *“If V is not an Object, and the conversion to an IDL value is being performed due to V being assigned to an attribute whose type is a nullable callback function that is annotated with [LegacyTreatNonObjectAsNull], then return the IDL nullable type T? value null.”* A non-object returns null outright — no coercion, no `toString`, nothing to park on — so the brief's premise for building a step machine was spec-wrong, and the lane refuted it by fetching. **THE ATTRIBUTE IS DECLARED IN ONE STANDARD AND HONOURED IN ANOTHER'S NULLABLE-TYPE SECTION, WHICH IS WHY §Do-subproblems-IN-ORDER APPLIES TO READING AS WELL AS TO BUILDING**: the order is DECLARATION, then CONVERSION, then the member's own steps, and starting at the third reads a value the first two already replaced. **AND THE DEEPER REASON THIS ONE GOT THROUGH IS THAT IT CARRIED NO SECTION NUMBER AT ALL, WHICH EVADES EVERY MECHANISM IN THIS FILE.** §A-SECTION-NUMBER-IN-A-CHECKLIST-IS-STILL-A-CLAIM closes the case where a number is asked about rather than asserted; this is one step further out — a claim ABOUT A STANDARD with no number in it, phrased as an explanation. `citegen` reads citations and finds nothing to check; the fetch-before-you-write rule triggers on numbers and never fires; and to a reader it looks like REASONING rather than like a claim, so it is not even a candidate for verification. It is also the form a coordinator produces most, because a brief is prose. **THE TELL IS A SUBORDINATE CLAUSE BEGINNING “SINCE” OR “BECAUSE” WHOSE OBJECT IS A SPEC** — that clause is a citation with its number removed, and it is owed the same `curl` the number would have been. **AND BEFORE WRITING AN AUDITOR FOR A PROPERTY, ASK WHETHER A CORPUS ALREADY IN THIS TREE TESTS IT — AN ORACLE YOU HAVE BEATS ONE YOU BUILD, AND IT IS ALREADY WRITTEN, ALREADY MAINTAINED, AND NOT YOURS TO BE WRONG ABOUT.** A member-list audit answers which members EXIST, and ARITY is an axis it has been measured not to ask — `grep -c variadic` answering 0 across every IDL tool in this tree — with the consequence that an interface printed `complete` over a wrong signature and a 468-site arity defect was invisible to every gate here. **AND THE SENTENCE THAT STOOD HERE SAID THAT INSTRUMENT ASKS `NOTHING ABOUT WHAT THEY ACCEPT`, WHICH WAS TRUE, WENT STALE, AND IS THE ONE DIRECTION THIS FILE RATES SILENT.** It was a claim about a TOOL'S CURRENT CAPABILITY standing in a file whose opening forbids status, and it rotted the moment that tool gained a dictionary-shaped accept audit — a whole finding category it now reports. That is an UNDER-CLAIM, so §AN-UNDER-CLAIM-IS-NOT-FOUND-BY-ACTING-ON-IT applies exactly: nobody discovers it by obeying it, because obeying it means NOT LOOKING THERE, and a reader who wants that axis goes and builds a second instrument beside the one already asking. Its neighbouring count rotted with it — `all three IDL tools` was four. **SO THE DURABLE FORM IS THE QUESTION AND NEVER THE INVENTORY: ASK WHICH AXIS AN INSTRUMENT OWNS BY RUNNING IT, and read its own verdict for what it says it could not see.**  WPT's `idlharness.js` — checked out, present, with `idlharness*` files across a dozen areas — reads `.length` off every member and diffs the whole surface against the published IDL. It is the oracle for exactly the property no local instrument could see. **AND THE AXIS IT OWNS IS NOT THE ONE THIS PARAGRAPH GUESSED, WHICH IS WHY THE RULE IS THE RULE AND THE EXAMPLE WAS STATUS.** Pointed at this engine, arity came back a ROUNDING ERROR — a handful of findings — while the population it actually names is what an installed member IS: property ATTRIBUTES, accessor-vs-data, the `[[Prototype]]` chain, `[Exposed]`, and BRAND CHECKS. The attribute half is per-INSTALLER rather than per-interface and is wrong in BOTH directions at once, which no per-interface audit could have shaped: quickjs's `JS_SetPropertyFunctionList` gives ECMAScript-builtin attributes where §3.7.6/§3.7.7 require `[[Enumerable]]: true`, and an ordinary `[[Set]]` onto the global gives enumerable where an interface object must not be — each exactly right for `Array.prototype.map` and exactly wrong for IDL. The brand half is worse than a wrong answer and is this file's own §WHOSE-BYTES-STATE-THE-VALUE violated: a receiver is PAGE-SUPPLIED INPUT, so a `DCHECK` on it hands any page an abort switch, and `Interface.prototype.method.call(null)` ABORTED THE ENGINE where Web IDL requires a TypeError — which a FORCING solver meets constantly, because calling with unusual receivers is what it does. So the durable instruction is to ask which AXIS an instrument owns and to run the corpus rather than predict its findings; the specific gaps above are status and will move. The tell that you are about to build a redundant auditor: the property you want to check is stated IN THE IDL rather than in this codebase's own vocabulary, which is precisely what a conformance corpus exists to check. **AND A PROPOSED NARROWING IS A HYPOTHESIS ABOUT A POPULATION YOU HAVE NOT COUNTED, SO IT IS BUILT AS A CLASSIFIER AND RUN OVER THAT POPULATION BEFORE IT IS LANDED — NEVER AFTER.** The usual way an auditor gets weakened is not carelessness, it is ONE FALSE POSITIVE READ AS A MECHANISM: you meet an accusing row, establish it is wrong, notice a property of the way it is WRITTEN, and propose that property as the discriminator. The reasoning is sound about the row and says NOTHING WHATEVER about what the rule EXCLUDES — a false positive proves the rule admits at least one, and the question you actually need answered is how many TRUE accusations share the same spelling. **AND THE COST IS ASYMMETRIC AND STRUCTURALLY INVISIBLE**: a retired true accusation is an §AN-UNDER-CLAIM, which nobody discovers by acting on it, because acting on it means not looking there. So the check cannot be deferred to review — it has to be a MEASUREMENT, and the measurement is cheap: implement the proposed rule as a classifier, run it over every row it would touch, and READ WHAT IT TAKES WITH IT. Measured, and the coordinator was me: I generalised one wrong row into a grammatical discriminator and dispatched it. Built as a classifier over its whole population, the rule retired **95 of 213** rows, and a stricter reading of the same rule still retired 66 — carrying off, among others, a real renumbering cluster where a file cites one ECMAScript section by a title belonging to another. Two rows that are GRAMMATICALLY IDENTICAL — same possessive, same run-on noun, same term defined elsewhere in the same standard — split one true and one false, and no property of the English separates them. **Four of the five probes in the lane's own control were possessives and were CORRECT accusations**, which is the whole refutation in one line. What replaced it was derived from the tool's own artifact rather than from prose: the file's comment claimed one check applied the identical rule as its neighbour, the neighbour tested containment in ONE direction and the comment's own site tested BOTH, and the missing half was on the ACCUSING side. That is a symmetry a file ASSERTED and its code did not have — checkable, landable, 13 rows retired and 0 introduced with the judged count held. **THE TELL THAT YOU ARE ABOUT TO DO THIS: your evidence for a narrowing is an EXAMPLE, and you cannot say how many rows share its shape.** **AND THE SECOND HALF OF THAT SAME ERROR IS READING A MECHANISM OUT OF A TOOL'S OWN EXPLANATION OF ITS OUTPUT.** An instrument that says *these sites cite a number whose OTHER sites are wrong above* is explaining why it PRINTED a group, and that is not a claim about what it CHECKED. Read as causation it becomes a suppression cascade — a second, larger subproblem, dispatched as such — and the code populates that list BEFORE the filter runs and independently of it, so retiring rows made the listing SHRINK rather than grow. **A tool's prose about its own output describes a PRESENTATION unless you have read the line that builds the list**, and the two are routinely written by the same author in opposite orders. Build the auditor only for what the corpus CANNOT ask — installation constructs, engine-side declarations, this fork's own invariants **AND A CORPUS IS EVIDENCE, NOT TRUTH — SO WHERE A PASS IS LOAD-BEARING, READ THE ASSERTION BEFORE TRUSTING IT.** Preferring an oracle you have to one you build is right because it is already written and already maintained; it does not make it correct, and importing it wholesale imports its blind spots as certainty. An external corpus can carry a VACUOUS check exactly as this tree can — two subtests that read the same object answer one question twice — and a component that passes both then reads as doubly confirmed while one axis was never examined. Measured, by a lane that read the harness rather than its result: an interface-constant test names a PROTOTYPE subtest and an INTERFACE-OBJECT subtest, and both take the descriptor off the interface object, so a constant correct on the constructor and wrong on the prototype passes both — only the separate value assertion could catch it. That is precisely the hole a per-target install-site check closes, and it was invisible from the outside: the pass count is identical either way. So audit the ASSERTION for the property you are actually claiming, quote which axis the corpus verified, and where the oracle cannot separate two targets say so rather than reporting its pass as coverage of both. — and when a gate's output is BYTE-IDENTICAL before and after a fix, say so plainly: that result is consistent with the fix AND with no fix, and naming what would actually confirm it is part of reporting it. **AND AN AUDITOR DERIVES THE RULE IT CHECKS FROM THE CODE THAT OWNS IT, NEVER RESTATES IT — A RESTATED RULE IS A SECOND COPY, AND THE ONE THAT DRIFTS IS THE COPY NOBODY RUNS AGAINST REALITY.** `idlgen` is trustworthy because it reads the real `.idl` through `webidl2` rather than a table of member names somebody typed; an auditor of this fork's own invariants earns the same standing only by parsing the declaration the engine already obeys. Worked example: the type-crossing audit derives its set of crossing types by **parsing `idl_concolic_rule`'s own switch**, so the day a type is added the audit knows, and a hand-kept list would have been the third copy of a fact whose second copy is what the audit exists to catch. **AND THE AXIS IS THE POINT, BECAUSE AUDITORS PARTITION AND EACH IS BLIND WHERE THE OTHERS LOOK**: names, argument COUNTS, and argument TYPES are three questions, and every one of the three here was built only after a defect walked past the other two — a wrong signature under `DOMTokenList: complete`, a `remove` that survived a length audit because its length was right, and seventy string coercions no member-list diff could see. So when an auditor reports zero, the honest sentence names the axis it asked about; and when a defect escapes every instrument, the question is which axis has no instrument yet, not which instrument was wrong. **AND AN AUDITOR KEYED ON WHAT EXISTS CANNOT COUNT WHAT DOES NOT EXIST AT ALL, WHICH MAKES ITS WORST FINDINGS THE ONES IT STRUCTURALLY OMITS.** A member-list diff walks the INTERFACES it can find and reports the members each is missing, so an interface with NO ROW contributes ZERO — and a wholly absent interface is the LOUDEST failure available to a page, a `ReferenceError` on the first line that names it. The count therefore reads as a work queue while systematically omitting the top of it. Measured: an audit reporting 445 absent members had no row for `CustomEvent`, `ResizeObserver`, `PerformanceObserver`, `EventSource`, `WebSocket`, `Worker`, `OffscreenCanvas`, `DOMMatrix`, `FontFace`, `ImageData`, `PointerEvent` or `DragEvent`, every one of which a real bundle constructs by name. **AND THE SAME AUDIT OVER-REPORTS AT ITS OTHER EDGE, WHICH IS THE HALF THAT LOOKS LIKE RIGOUR**: it walks one directory, so a member another layer installs is reported ABSENT while a page reaches it perfectly well — `btoa`/`atob` are listed missing from `Window` and both answer, because the JS engine installs them outside the audited tree. So a per-component walk is blind in BOTH directions at its boundaries, and neither error announces itself. **THE CURE IS TO ASK THE ENGINE RATHER THAN THE AUDIT**: enumerate the platform's own global names and test each for presence in a real realm, which is one document and answers about the program that actually runs. Measured, 3/3 identical runs: **883 of 1333 platform global names absent**, against the audit's 445 members — a different and much larger surface, ranked by counting unambiguous construction sites (`new X(`, `window.X`, `X instanceof`) in real frozen bundles rather than by interface size. The `.member` channel is unusable for that ranking and must be discarded rather than weighted: in minified code `.name`, `.length` and `.replace` collide with ordinary object properties. **AND TWO AUDITORS THAT BOTH OBEY THIS RULE CAN STILL CONTRADICT EACH OTHER — THE DERIVED READER IS INVISIBLE TO THE NAME-GREP ONE, SO THE FIELD FLAGGED AS UNREAD IS THE ONE WHOSE READER IS BUILT CORRECTLY.** The rule above says an auditor parses the declaration its subject already obeys; a consumer that does the same thing has NO LITERAL TOKEN for the field it consumes, and an auditor asking "is this name read anywhere" then answers NO about a reader that requires the field on every run and throws when it is absent. The two are both right about their own axis and the report reads as a defect, which is the direction that costs something: **the prescribed remedy for a write-with-no-reader is to add a reader, and adding a literal one here RE-CREATES THE HAND-TYPED LIST THE DERIVATION REPLACED** — a list this tree records as having been wrong SEVEN times, with three of its misses landing in the hour the derivation that ended it was being written. So the auditor's finding does not merely mislead, it argues for undoing the fix. Measured: a census composer's rows were reported as six field names "WRITTEN with no reader", and every one of the six was in the required set the reader DERIVES from that composer's own format string — checked by slicing the derivation out and running it, which took one command and answered `true`. **THE TELL IS THAT THE FLAGGED NAMES ARE A CONTIGUOUS FAMILY FROM ONE PRODUCER RATHER THAN SCATTERED**, because a real broken contract is one name somebody renamed on one side, while a derivation covers a producer WHOLE — so six rows of one census reading unread together is a statement about the auditor and not about the census. Before acting on a write-with-no-reader, ASK WHETHER THE CONSUMER DERIVES ITS SET, and run the derivation rather than grepping for the name; and an auditor that cannot answer that question reports the band as UNAUDITED, never as a defect, for the reason the same tool already bands "ambiguous" and "refused" apart from its findings — a count that mixes "I found a defect" with "I cannot see this construct" is the three-states-behind-one-answer shape, **AND THE BAND THAT HONESTLY SAYS `I CANNOT DECIDE THIS` IS THEREFORE THE WORST PLACE TO SEND A LANE, BECAUSE IT IS ENRICHED FOR FALSE POSITIVES BY CONSTRUCTION.** Banding is right and this is its consequence: an instrument that separates what it CAN decide from what it CANNOT has, by that very act, swept the undecidable into one place — so the band is defined by the tool's blindness rather than by the tree's defects, and the defects go on living in the channels where the tool DID decide and said so. Scoping by the loudest band therefore INVERTS the yield: you spend the lane on the accusations that are wrong and never open the ones that are right. Measured twice, in two components, by two lanes that did not share a reading: a lane read 31 findings across six files and **every one of the 21 entries in the unseparable band was this tree's own prose — not one fabrication — while every genuine spec defect came from an ADJACENT channel**, the ones naming a mis-transcription, a mis-attribution or an out-of-range step. The second lane read 11 more in two other files and found the same thing — **32 of 32 band entries own prose, zero fabrications across both** — while its defects came from the adjacent channels AND from four sites no channel could report at all, because a citation carrying no quotation has nothing for a quotation check to fail on. **THAT LAST PART IS THE HARDER HALF AND IT IS WHY THE BAND IS NOT MERELY A POOR INDEX BUT A MISLEADING ONE**: it found a section number wrong at FIVE sites, one of them inside a `DCHECK` string, and exactly ONE of the five was reportable by any channel — the rest carried no quotation, so an instrument had nothing to judge and said nothing. A component can therefore be uniformly wrong about which section governs it while every channel reads zero, and the only thing that finds it is reading the file against the standard. **AND A THIRD LANE REFUTED THE ABSOLUTE ONE HOUR AFTER IT WAS WRITTEN, WHICH IS THE PART TO KEEP.** `32 of 32` was true of two components and is NOT a property of the band: a third read nine more and found **7 own prose and 2 GENUINE** — one a standard's verbatim sentence whose nearest-preceding citation named a different standard entirely, one a real sentence with this tree's prefix and emphasis welded on, cited at a section that does not define the term. **BOTH DIVERGED AT WORD ONE OR TWO, WHICH IS EXACTLY WHAT OWN PROSE DOES**, so the tell everyone was using — an early divergence means we wrote it — is worthless in precisely the case that matters. The refinement is one question and it is cheap: **an entry whose text READS LIKE A STANDARD is checked against a DIFFERENT SECTION before it is called own prose**, because the divergence word tells you nothing when the ANCHOR is wrong; that is the correct-quotation-of-a-non-governing-section reading wearing the own-prose reading's clothes. What survived all three lanes is the narrower claim and the useful one: **zero fabrications in 41 entries**, and the band as a WORK INDEX is still inverted — that lane's own file split 7 own-prose to 10 genuine, so scoping by the band would have found the seven and missed the ten. Keep the direction, drop the absolute: this file asserted `not one fabrication` as a count over two components and it was a count over two components, which is what an absolute always turns out to be. **THE TELL IS THAT A BAND'S NAME DESCRIBES THE INSTRUMENT AND NOT THE SUBJECT** — `unseparable`, `unjudgeable`, `ambiguous`, `refused`, `no root reaches it` are all confessions, and a confession is a poor index of where anything is wrong. **AND THE LISTING AN INSTRUMENT EXPLICITLY DOES NOT ACCUSE IS WORSE THAN THE BAND, BECAUSE AN UNRESOLVED CITATION SUPPRESSES EVERY CHECK BENEATH IT.** A band at least reports something a reader can dismiss; a `not accused` listing is where the tool has said in advance that nothing here is a finding — and if what makes an entry land there is that its citation resolves to NOTHING, then no quotation under it was ever compared, no title was ever matched, and the silence is the absence of a question rather than the absence of a defect. **THE POPULATION UNDER SUCH A ROW IS UN-AUDITED, NOT CLEAN**, and it is the one place in a well-instrumented tree where a defect can sit indefinitely with every channel reading zero. Measured: 26 sites carrying a bare section number that resolved to a section of a standard that has no such subsection, filed as number-only and never accused. Seven were read: they meant THREE DIFFERENT SECTIONS of two different standards, and two of them were real defects — a document's encoding and two DOM tree accessors — invisible precisely because nothing under an unresolvable citation is ever checked. **AND THE MECHANICAL REPAIR IS THE TRAP**: prefixing all 26 with the one standard they appeared to mean would have made them resolve, which would have CERTIFIED both defects rather than exposing them. So a number-only or unresolved listing is read ENTRY BY ENTRY or left alone, never swept — the entries share a spelling and not a referent, which is why the tool refused to guess in the first place. The tell that you are about to sweep one: you have a repair that applies uniformly to every row of a list whose rows the instrument declined to distinguish. So read EVERY channel a per-file run reports and let the tool's own confident verdicts lead; the band is a queue of things to CORRECT THE RECORD about, which is worth doing and is not the same work as finding defects. And when you hand such a band on, say which it is., arriving in the instrument built to end it. **AND THE MIRROR OF THAT SHAPE IS EVIDENCE INFLATION: N ROWS READING 0 ARE NOT N SIGNALS WHEN ONE OF THEM IS A CONJUNCTION OF THE OTHERS.** Everything above is one answer hiding several states. This is several answers that are really ONE FACT, and it is the more flattering error — a reader counting rows believes the evidence is corroborated when it is a single observation printed three times. A row defined as `a && b` is ENTAILED by either conjunct's zero, so it carries no information whatever about the mechanism and cannot corroborate anything; it is not a weak signal, it is the same signal with a different name. **THE TELL IS CHEAP AND IT IS AN ABSENCE OF PROSE**: a derived row usually has no explanation of its own, because there is nothing to explain — it prints as a bare digit beside the rows it is made of, while every genuine row around it carries a reason. So when a table shows several zeros, READ EACH ROW'S DEFINITION BEFORE COUNTING THEM, and a row that is a function of its neighbours is reported as one fact with its derivation named, never as an independent confirmation. Measured, and it happened in a coordinator's own brief: three probe rows were relayed to a lane as three mechanism readings at 0, and the third was a strict conjunction containing the second — two facts dispatched as three, with the inflated count doing the work of making the case look settled. **THE CURE IS THE SAME ONE §a-bare-count-over-a-population-you-have-not-partitioned PRESCRIBES, APPLIED TO THE ROWS INSTEAD OF THE MEMBERS**: give the derived row the reason its neighbours have — here, the forwarded text of whichever conjunct is zero — so the entailment is visible in the output rather than recoverable only by reading the definitions.
- **Headless is not valueless — MODEL the state with mock-backed IO, don't shrug to opaque.** The only missing piece is a physical IO device; the spec still defines behavior without one. File System Access presents a MOCK filesystem that ROUND-TRIPS — `file_system.c` is the FS Standard's own model (file/directory entries, binary data, the lock and its shared count), reached through `storage_manager.c`'s bucket root and `file_system_handle.c`, and a byte written through a `FileSystemWritableFileStream` is a byte a later `getFile().text()` reads back. This line used to point at a `storage.c` that has never existed in this tree, which is why the file it named must be one you can open; `HTMLMediaElement` is a real state machine that plays with no device; `matchMedia` resolves a default viewport for its `.matches` example yet stays CONCOLIC (a branch still forks the alternate-viewport world); `getComputedStyle` layers UA-default + inline; `AbortSignal` is the real aborted-state machine; a `[CEReactions]` op RUNS custom-element reactions. Opaque is reserved for the UNKNOWABLE (attacker input, server-injected absent state), never for "there is no device." Never collapse a modelable value to bare-concrete either — that deletes the fork and its coverage; the concolic value is opaque-for-control-flow yet carries the concrete example. Err toward MORE exploration (the WFQ starves the unproductive arm).

# Solver half — the NOVEL contribution: forced multi-path concolic execution on time-travel primitives
- **Every value is CONCOLIC — a triple of (source-identity provenance, a constraint-DOMAIN of what it can be, a concrete EXAMPLE when the code pins/computes/learns one). There is no binary "opaque" sentinel** — that oversimplification is deleted. "Unknown external input" is the MOST GENERAL concolic value: unconstrained domain, no example yet, tagged by source. The solver's whole job is to NARROW the domain and FILL the example. Every predicate narrows (`x=='p'`, `startsWith('http')`, `<3`); every op propagates the example (`"/api/"+cfg.region ⇒ /api/us-east-1`, never `/api/{region}`). The example propagates because the engine RUNS THE REAL OP on the concrete (the `+` actually concatenates) — NOT because a carried label/note is derived by a propagation hook. The triple RIDES the value the interpreter already computes; it is not a second value-class shadowing it. A separate tagged-value class whose result-note a `Combine`-style hook derives at each op IS the recorded transform-expression §Re-execution forbids (it cannot see interprocedural/shared-mutable state), a redundant WORSE taint tracker beside the sound re-execution search — building one is the cardinal misread of this line, not an implementation of it. `JS_IsConcolic` (domain-carrying) is the only predicate; a re-introduced know-nothing branch is a regression.
- **Forced multi-path (J-Force lineage) FUSED with a per-flow path constraint (DART/SAGE/Kudzu lineage).** Concrete execution is the GROUND TRUTH that grounds all shared/interprocedural state, so the constraint is only ever over INPUT variables — which is why "a solver can't model shared state" is a category error (the solver never sees state). Control flow is FEASIBLE-REFINED by the domain, not blind fork-both: where the domain permits both outcomes BOTH arms run (reaching gated/admin code); a contradicted branch is pruned (sound-only — uncertainty keeps the arm). **CONCRETIZE-ON-PIN**: once `x==='admin'` pins the value, a later READ of that source returns the pinned bytes, so a later branch on it is decided by RUNNING the real predicate on a real string and does not fork at all — the pin is a fact about the VALUE and the concretization is the interpreter's ordinary work, never a second evaluator that re-decides predicates from a recorded constraint. **AND THAT SENTENCE NAMES TWO PRECONDITIONS THAT DO NOT TRAVEL WHEN THE PRIMITIVE IS CITED AS PRECEDENT, WHICH IS HOW A WRONG MECHANISM GETS BUILT WITH THIS FILE'S OWN AUTHORITY BEHIND IT.** A pin is sound because the determination comes from a predicate THE RUNNING FLOW ITSELF EVALUATED, and because it is about THE VALUE BEING MINTED. Any proposal of the form "this is concretize-on-pin one level up" must carry both, and the phrase itself is the tell: it asserts a likeness while naming no shared precondition, and it transfers the primitive's standing to a mechanism that may share neither. Measured, and it was a coordinator who did it to a lane: a design was anchored here that would retire an already-minted arm when a SIBLING arm established the program defines a global. Both preconditions fail — the fact comes from another arm rather than from the flow being narrowed, and it DELETES A WORLD rather than determining a value — and `window.__FLAGS = window.__FLAGS || {}`, the ordinary spelling of every SSR hydration shim, is the counterexample: the surviving arm holds a bundle-built empty object whose extent no channel admits, so the auth gate answers concretely and the admin surface is buried, which is the exact loss the component exists to prevent. **THE REFUTATION WAS ALREADY WRITTEN AT THE PRIMITIVE AND NOBODY READ IT BEFORE CITING IT** — the pin's own site says the witness "does not merely get emitted, it deletes the sibling world the run never contradicted", and that is the proposed mechanism described in advance. So the rule is mechanical: **before offering a primitive as precedent, read that primitive's own hazard notes**, because a component that has been got wrong once documents the way it goes wrong, and an analogy is exactly the move that skips them. **AND THE CATEGORY THIS LANDS IN IS NOT `cap`, IT IS WORSE THAN ONE.** §NO BOUNDS forbids deciding work will not happen, and the reflex defence of a retirement is that it NARROWS rather than bounds — which is true of a narrowing whose premise the run FALSIFIED, and false here: defining a global does not contradict "an external agent supplied it", it is compatible with it. A cap defers work that can still happen and announces itself as a limit; a WRONG NARROWING deletes a world nothing contradicted, keeps no arm to resume, and looks like the search getting sharper. So the question asked of every arm-retirement is not "is this a bound" but **"which observation FALSIFIED the premise of the arm being retired"** — and if the answer is a fact merely CONSISTENT with the arm's premise, it is not evidence, and the retirement is the wrong pin one level up. Symbolic contradiction detection is only the unpinned residue (an open interval like `x>5 then x<3`), keyed by the PREDICATE's own identity — operator and both operands — so the flow's record of one predicate decides that predicate and never its neighbour. The scheduler asks the substrate ONE feasibility seam and holds zero constraint logic.
- **COUNTING A WORLD'S COMPLETIONS BY ENUMERATING A SPEC ALGORITHM'S ARMS UNDERCOUNTS, BECAUSE A WORLD THE ALGORITHM NEVER REACHES IS NOT ONE OF ITS ARMS — AND A FORK BUILT OVER THAT COUNT DELETES IT.** This is the wrong-narrowing defect arriving through ARITHMETIC rather than through a premise, and it is harder to see because the count is derived from the standard itself, which is exactly where this file says to derive things from. You read the algorithm, you find a normal arm and an abrupt arm, you write two — and the abrupt arm is a SETTLEMENT like the normal one, so the world in which the algorithm settles NOTHING is a completion the algorithm's own arms do not name. Measured: an assert said to fork the call of an unknown thenable "over its two completions", and the third is the ordinary `{then(){}}` a browser really does leave pending for ever. A two-way fork would have deleted the one world the page can actually be in when nothing settles. **THE TELL IS THAT THE COUNT HAD NEVER BEEN BUILT.** It said two for as long as it was PROSE, and was found wrong the moment the same fork was needed one stage up, where the completions had to be enumerated in order to exist. A number in a comment is unfalsifiable until something has to construct the things it counts — so a count that has only ever been asserted is a claim awaiting its first reader, and the reader who has to build it is the one who finds out. So: when an assert or a comment states HOW MANY arms a fork owes, ask what happens in the world where the algorithm returns without settling, without calling, without resuming — and if that world is real, it is an arm. The cheap check is to name each completion as an OBSERVABLE outcome rather than as a branch of the text: "resolve called", "reject called", "neither called" is a partition of what the page can see, while "normal arm, abrupt arm" is a partition of what the prose happens to enumerate.
