All three are about the REQUEST and none about WHO DECIDED TO SEND IT, which is the entire error and the reason it will be re-derived. So a reader who finds that relay ungated has found a DECISION, not a gap, and the honest response to "one sanctioned transport stands on no floor" is that the floor under an operator's act is the operator seeing it. **AND AN EXEMPTION SCOPED BY WHO ACTED IS SCOPED BY A FACT THE EXEMPTING SITE CANNOT SEE, SO IT IS CARRIED AND ASSERTED OR IT IS NOT SCOPED AT ALL.** That is the half this paragraph got wrong, and it got it wrong in the most durable way available: by describing the CALL GRAPH. It said the relay's entries were operator-typed or an operator-initiated probe, which was a claim about which functions happened to call which, written in a file where nothing could check it — and it was FALSE of three callers the whole time it stood, each firing the instant a captured response body arrived, each credentialed by construction, two of them POSTing a body the app never produced. The relay could see the URL, the verb, the headers and the body, and those are IDENTICAL in shape whether a person typed them or a response handler composed them; so there was nothing there to notice, and the exemption quietly covered the exact population it was argued not to cover. **THE DEFECT CLASS IS AN INVARIANT WHOSE SUBJECT IS ABSENT FROM THE SITE THAT RELIES ON IT** — every other rule in this file about a fact that must travel (the browser-stated principal, the request's provenance, the sender's origin on a delivered message, the initiator here) is the same shape, and the cure is always the same one: the fact is a VALUE stated by the site that knows, carried down every frame, and asserted where it is relied on, never re-derived and never inferred. The tell that you are writing one: the justification for a privilege is a sentence about who the callers are. **AND THE CALLER THAT CANNOT STATE THE FACT IS THE POINT, NOT THE FRICTION.** When the grade became a required value, the three automatic callers had nowhere to go — the chokepoint enforces §9.2.1 STRUCTURALLY, by hardcoding `GET` and reading neither the method nor the body, so a POST error probe cannot be routed there and must not be given a way — and that is the correct outcome rather than an obstacle: the capability moved to the grade entitled to it (a button naming the endpoint), the automatic form was DELETED rather than exempted, and the reach that was genuinely lost was reported as lost. An unstated value takes the same arm as the refused one, so forgetting is not a way to be exempted. **AND IT LIVES IN `safe-fetch.js` WITH EVERY OTHER RISK DECISION** — the engine holds no network policy by construction, so the deny list, the credential choice and the firing decision are ONE policy at ONE chokepoint, and a second copy inside the engine would be the layering violation §Learning-from-replies already names. What the engine owes instead is honesty about what it LEANED ON: a fired safe-method request rests on §9.2.1's contract, and a finding derived from one states that it PRESUMED rather than implying it verified. That presumption is exactly what the per-origin setting above configures, because whether a given app honors the contract is the one thing its owner knows and the engine never can.
- **A REAL NAVIGABLE IS A LEGITIMATE INSTRUMENT, AND THE LINE IS NOT MODELLED-VERSUS-REAL — IT IS WHOSE PRINCIPAL THE NAVIGATION CARRIES.** An earlier form of this rule said exploration must never materialize a real browsing artifact. That drew the line on the wrong axis and foreclosed the capability this tool exists for: a bundle NAMES pages a person never visits — a route only its own router knows, an admin view no link reaches — and reaching them is §What-the-tool-produces' whole subject ("what the bundle CAN do but didn't"), while §LIVE-VERIFY already calls a real fire "ground-truth real exploit" and delivers it through a real popup. A real artifact was therefore never the forbidden thing, and a rule that banned it was protecting nothing while costing the product its reach. What is forbidden is ACTING AS THE PERSON WITHOUT THEIR SAY, and exactly one property decides that: a top-level navigation in their profile carries their cookies, which is what lets a real tab perform an account action and learn a credentialed reply no logged-out client could see — the two hazards §provenance names, arriving through the browser instead of through `fetch`. So the instrument is one of the CUSTOM BROWSER'S OWN TABS. **ITS TABS AND WINDOW REFERENCES ARE ITS OWN CONCEPT AND HAVE NOTHING TO DO WITH CHROME'S** — no `chrome.tabs`, no browser window, no handle that outlives the engine that made it; a navigable dies with its agent, which is why nothing here needs a durable tab record, a browser-session epoch or a startup reconciliation, and why an earlier version of this bullet that reached for all three was answering a question that does not arise. **EVERY ONE OF THEM LOADS THROUGH `safeFetch`** — there is no second transport, and the chokepoint SECURITY.md names has NO carve-out for navigation. **AND EVERY ONE OF THEM SENDS COOKIES**: `safeFetch` supports credentialed loads, and a SAME-ORIGIN navigation carries them, exactly as a browser's does. There is no credential-free context, no partition, no incognito, and no interception — `declarativeNetRequest` is not used in this project at all. That is not a concession extracted from the design; it is what makes a tab worth having and what keeps it honest. A SESSION-LESS TAB IS A THIRD THING THAT MODELS NOTHING: not the person's browser and not a clean client, served a bundle the person will never be served, producing findings that do not reproduce for them — which is exactly what §Attacker-sources means when it says a source stripped of its real constraints yields PoCs that don't reproduce. And a credential-stripping rule would be a GUARD where §Fix-the-ROOT wants an impossible state — safe only while installed and correct, with the window between the tab and the rule as the harm — while placing a NETWORK POLICY where `safeFetch` cannot see it, a second policy point beside the one SECURITY.md names as the only one.
  **WHICH PUTS THE WHOLE OF THE SAFETY IN THE CHOICE OF ADDRESS, DECIDED BEFORE THE NAVIGATION, WITH NO SECOND LINE BEHIND IT.** That is a stronger claim than it looks and it is what the rest of this bullet rests on. A TAB IS A VISIT, NOT AN EXECUTION SURFACE: forced execution happens in the WASM sandbox, and a tab exists to obtain a document and to host §LIVE-VERIFY, so a legitimately-chosen address makes a real tab exactly what it appears to be — this extension visiting a page the way the person would. A top-level navigation is a GET, which RFC 9110 §9.2.1 Safe Methods' safe set contains, so the METHOD half is already answered and the entire remaining question is PROVENANCE. An OBSERVED or DERIVED address is one the app's own code computed from real inputs and a person could have reached, and it is navigated freely — that IS the capability: reaching what a bundle NAMES and no link exposes. A FORCED address is the deliberate per-origin widening, and its reply is carried as FORCED and never merged into the observed pool, because a CREDENTIALED reply to a navigation no client would make is the plausible fabrication §provenance is entirely about. A navigation whose provenance is not established CRASHES at the decision rather than proceeding: with no partition and no interception there is nothing downstream to catch it, so the assert belongs at the only control point that exists. The forbidden triple is unchanged and is now reachable by exactly one route — the address. **AND "ALL NETWORK VIA `safeFetch`" STAYS LITERALLY TRUE, TRANSPORT INCLUDED.** A navigation is a LOAD like any other and goes through the chokepoint; an earlier form of this bullet carved navigation out of that invariant and reread it as a statement about the DECISION rather than the transport, and that was the whole of the error. What would create a second transport is reaching for a BROWSER-DRIVEN tab, and that is the inversion it looks like: every guarantee `safeFetch` makes is decided at its call site — the scheme allowlist, the SOP/CORS gate, PNA on the initial AND the post-redirect URL, CORB by expected type — so a path that skips them is not a more faithful browser, it is an UNPOLICED one. The custom browser's own tab is the faithful one precisely because its bytes come through the same door as every other byte.
- **A DOCUMENT REACHED IN A CYCLE DOES NOT BREAK THE SCHEDULER — IT IS THE CASE THE WFQ IS FOR — BUT THE ADDRESS-VARYING CYCLE DEFEATS THE ARGUMENT THAT SAYS SO, AND THAT DIFFERENCE IS THE WHOLE ANSWER.** A page that frames a page that frames it back, or a page a person opens ten times, APPENDS flows to the ONE frontier — it starts no scheduler, no run and no counter, so there is nothing there to break. A cycle over the SAME address is then handled by the mechanism that already handles iterating an opaque collection: the repeat emits nothing new, the reward term stops paying, CPU-aging sinks it beneath productive and unrun work, and it pages to the cold tier — OUTRANKED, never terminated, and a seen-set to "fix" it is the banned cap. **The cycle that varies the ADDRESS is a different shape and must not be waved past with the same sentence**: a cache-busting query makes every turn a NEW document identity whose first visit legitimately emits, so "the identical tail emits nothing new" is simply FALSE of it and the demotion argument does not apply to it at all. Its answer is the one the disk share already gives — residency is the STRICT PREFIX of the one value order, so a cycle's near-identical documents compete on VALUE against everything else and lose residency as their marginal emission decays, while MEMBERSHIP is untouched. And membership stays affordable for the reason the re-derivable category names: a child address a loop COMPUTED is re-derivable by re-running the parent that computed it, so shedding a generated recipe converts storage into recomputation and truncates nothing. What such a cycle genuinely threatens is neither the queue nor the disk but the REALM CEILING — it is the sharpest generator of per-flow navigables there is, which makes it the FORCING FUNCTION for §7.3.1.6 Navigable destruction's destroy-a-navigable, never a reason to bound the loop.
- **PASSIVE AND FORCED DISCOVERY COMBINE AS A COMPARISON AND NEVER AS A MERGE — and the merge is the tempting one, because it makes the report look FULLER while the solver gets WORSE.** "Passive discovery" names two things that must not share a fate, and separating them is the whole of the design. **WHAT THE PAGE'S OWN TRAFFIC REVEALS IS A THERMOMETER, NOT A SOURCE.** A passively observed endpoint is OBSERVED by construction — the page made that request — so pooling it with a derived one yields a single list in which this tool's own contribution is UNMEASURABLE, and §What-the-tool-produces already says why that is fatal: `netdiff --unused` is a DIAGNOSTIC that the solver dominates the live page, NEVER the optimization target. Merge them and the diagnostic reads the union of itself — an instrument measuring its own input — so the number rises, the driving rots, and NOTHING SAYS SO. That is the plausible-datum defect performed at the level of the product rather than a field. The rule above already fixes the direction: an endpoint learned from a runtime fetch/XHR hook rather than from QuickJS means the forced execution UNDERPERFORMED, so the passive channel's entire worth is that it can EMBARRASS the solver, and a design that merges it away has spent the only measurement that could. **WHAT A PERSON'S BROWSING REVEALS THAT NO SOLVER CAN DERIVE IS WHICH DOCUMENTS EXIST.** A solver explores the bundle it was handed; which origins someone reaches, in which app, behind which auth state, is not a fact inside any one document — so it is not a finding the forced execution failed to make, it is the SEED. That is the half an ambient host is worth keeping for, and it enters the ONE frontier as a document to explore, ranked like any other flow. **A SUGGESTION IS A WORK ITEM, NOT A FINDING**: passive observation may propose WHAT to explore and may never arrive in the emitted surface wearing the grade of something the run derived. **AND A SEED IS AN ADDRESS, NEVER BYTES — an ambient host that ships a DOCUMENT BODY has built a second document-load transport, which is the carve-out §the-chokepoint-has-no-carve-out forbids, wearing the costume of a convenience.** The failure is not duplication, it is that the second path is UNPOLICED BY CONSTRUCTION: bytes fetched in a content script are fetched in the page's own realm, with the person's cookies, reaching `safeFetch` never — so the scheme allowlist, the SOP/CORS gate, PNA on the initial and post-redirect URL, CORB by expected type and the destructive-path deny list all apply to the engine's own navigations and to nothing that arrives this way. It is also a SECOND CREDENTIALED FETCH of a document the person already loaded, which can legitimately answer differently (cache, nonce, personalised SSR, rate limit), so it does not even buy the observed bytes it appears to. What an ambient observer knows that nothing else can is exactly ONE FACT, and it is an address: **the URL the browser actually NAVIGATED to**, which `PerformanceNavigationTiming.name` states and which `history.pushState` cannot forge — the distinction that matters for an SPA, whose `location.href` is a client route the server would 404. Ship that, and the custom browser loads it as an HTML §7.4 navigation through the one chokepoint, with the policy read off the reply exactly as §7.4 step 14 requires. A justification that the content script's fetch uniquely sees response headers is FALSE where a host-permission fetch reads every header, and a rule kept alive by a reason that has stopped being true is the stale-comment failure with a network path attached.
- **AND ONCE THE SEED IS AN ADDRESS, THE SEED IS PER-ORIGIN AND NOT PER-VISIT — WHICH DECIDES THE VEHICLE.** Forced execution from an app's INDEX page reads the bundle, and the bundle NAMES the routes no link exposes, so one entry point per app yields the hidden pages, their endpoints and their sinks. That is the capability, and it means the frontier does not need a stream of every page a person opened — it needs the list of apps, which a person can state, paste, bookmark or import. **So an AMBIENT host is a very expensive way to deliver a string.** Weigh it honestly against what it costs, and the costs are measured rather than argued: a single-threaded WASM instance has no CPU clock and cannot be interrupted mid-call, so §scheduler's cooperative quantum — which that section calls REQUIRED and universal — has no asynchronous budget source at all; the SharedArrayBuffer that would supply one cannot be transferred into an opaque origin at ANY manifest setting, and the COOP flip that would change that severs the opener §LIVE-VERIFY needs; wasm32's address ceiling sits below the frontier's live allocation, so the ASan §Architecture prescribes by name cannot run on the shipped program; and a process boundary must be rebuilt inside a document because two instances in one realm are a namespace rather than a sandbox. Against that, the ambient half buys BREADTH someone would otherwise curate — a convenience, not a capability, and one a minimal feeder that ships only ADDRESSES can supply without any of the above. **The engine's home is therefore the host that gives it real threads, a real CPU clock, a real `fork()` and a real sanitizer; a browser vehicle is ONE host among several, driven through the same ABI, and never the thing every design decision is priced against.** What it must NOT become is a second engine: one ABI, one scheduler, one frontier, whichever host is running.
- **A SELF-SEEDED DOCUMENT IS A WORK ITEM ON THE ONE FRONTIER, AND THE ONLY THING THAT MAKES IT DIFFERENT FROM A FLOW IS THAT IT SPENDS SOMEONE ELSE'S SERVER.** Discovery that walks from an index page generates ADDRESSES, so the cycle question §a-document-reached-in-a-cycle answers arrives here by construction rather than by accident, and the answer is unchanged: ranked by emitted value, outranked when it stops emitting, resident as a strict prefix of the one value order — never a visited-set, a crawl depth, a page budget or a same-URL check, all of which are §NO BOUNDS' caps wearing a crawler's vocabulary. **RE-FETCHING AN ADDRESS IS NOT THE DEFECT AND MUST NOT BE PREVENTED**: §Time-travel-resume REQUIRES a resumed flow to re-derive its examples from CURRENT sources, and the re-derivable tier converts stored bytes into exactly this recomputation, so an engine that refuses to fetch an address twice has broken both. What the ordering owes the other side is that an outbound request is an EXTERNAL EFFECT and therefore a cost the WFQ carries like any other, so an unproductive document sinks beneath productive work instead of being re-fetched at the same rank for ever — the same starvation mechanism, applied to the one work item whose cost is not ours to spend. The UI that presents any of this is a SEPARATE AXIS and decides none of it — a surface can render both columns, and which host collects them is not a question about how they are rendered. **AND THAT CEILING IS THE SAME DEFECT WHETHER THE NAVIGABLE IS REAL OR MODELLED, so refusing real ones does not fix it — it only changes which resource runs out**, and the modelled resource is the engine's while the real one is the PERSON'S. That asymmetry does not make the cycle a different problem; it makes reclamation load-bearing sooner, and it is why destroy-a-navigable is the root fix rather than a policy about which kind of navigable exploration may build.
- **Bind before build:** source a missing Web/spec capability host-runtime (JSPI) → engine intrinsic → existing Lexbor module → faithful spec port → hand-roll last (never crypto/parsers from scratch). APIs must follow their IDLs; nothing may be opaque. APIs work regardless of multiple tabs/windows (cross-WASM chains), a real permission system, plugged-in speakers, or a real filesystem via virtualization. Treat the browser as REAL — permission-gated APIs, URL params, FSA, popups, iframes — or the project can't be made; virtual systems (their own component) let forced execution reach more code (an FSA file containing XSS).

# What the tool produces
Run the page's UNMODIFIED bundle on patched QuickJS (Lexbor DOM) under forced multi-path execution — one run, two views: API records AND XSS/security verdicts. Surface INTERESTING UNUSED endpoints (login/click/route/flag-gated, dead-but-shipped, lazy-chunk) with computed example KEYS and VALUES. A sniffer shows what FIRED; this shows what the bundle CAN do but didn't. **Ideal: learn the LOGGED-IN API surface WHILE LOGGED OUT** — SPAs ship the same bundle to logged-out visitors, so it contains the auth/admin/billing code that never fires without state. `harness netdiff --unused` (learned-not-live) is a DIAGNOSTIC that the solver dominates the live page, NOT the optimization target — optimize the solver's real-valued learned surface, never the diff. Target JS-heavy app/feature pages, not marketing pages. Rotate value areas (endpoint coverage, example values, required headers, resume loop, render-cycle, security/PoC, UI, perf, cross-session resume).

# Security → SECURITY.md
Authorization keys on `sender.tab.url` (never `sender.id`/`msg.*`); credentialed-read PRINCIPAL = `MessageSender.origin`, never URL-derived; content script + website + QuickJS/WASM bundle are UNTRUSTED, the offscreen is the only trusted zone — BUT THE ENGINE DOES NOT RUN IN IT. `bridge.js` says so in its own first paragraph ("NO ENGINE LIVES IN THIS REALM"): every instance is a RENDERER, an `<iframe sandbox="allow-scripts" src="renderer.html">` whose unique OPAQUE ORIGIN is the security boundary, reached over a mojom interface. Measured consequence: `self.crossOriginIsolated` is FALSE in that frame at both COOP values and with the sandbox attribute widened or removed, so a SharedArrayBuffer cannot be TRANSFERRED to it and the wasm host cannot have a watchdog thread. Pricing anything against the offscreen prices the wrong document; one WASM instance per ORIGIN-KEYED AGENT CLUSTER — `(browsing-context group, origin)`, which is what SECURITY.md §The QuickJS/WASM sandbox is attacker-controlled states and is NOT "per document", the wording that stood here and contradicted the bullet directly below it; ALL network via `safe-fetch.js` or `pageContextFetch`; state in the offscreen + IndexedDB (`chrome.storage.local` banned). Tabs may hold cross-origin documents.
- **AN INSTANCE IS AN ORIGIN-KEYED AGENT CLUSTER — `(browsing-context group, origin)` — because that IS the spec's heap boundary, and ACROSS one THE COW DELTA SYNCHRONISES.** Two same-origin documents in one browsing-context group are ONE similar-origin window agent: HTML puts them in a single heap and then RELIES on it. `iframe.contentDocument.body.appendChild(subframe)` inserts a node THIS document created (§4.5 adopt, then the insertion steps), so `subframe.parentNode` is afterwards a node belonging to the OTHER document while the wrapper stays the same object; `frame.contentWindow.onunload = fn` hands the other document this agent's live closure to CALL, with a live Event going back. Those are not property reads that could suspend — they are one object graph — and passing them by NAME is an unbounded bidirectional export table with cross-boundary GC that answers `===` wrong. Splitting a same-origin pair is therefore not the stricter choice, it is the WRONG one, and refusing to costs nothing: the security invariant is ORIGIN, not document, so SECURITY.md's one-principal-per-instance still holds EXACTLY, and an origin-keyed cluster is also what makes `document.domain` a no-op — spec behavior, not a hedge. What crosses an instance boundary is then a CLOSED set — WindowProxy, Location, serialized messages — which is the whole reason the boundary is tractable and is what a real engine ships (Blink's `RemoteDOMWindow`/`RemoteFrame`). A DIFFERENT-ORIGIN document, or the same origin in another browsing-context group, IS a separate instance and may be remote; a flow touching both still has ONE world spanning them, and the delta that world is made of must apply, unapply and park across all of them — a per-instance delta that only agrees locally is two timelines wearing one name, the identical bug class as modelling async state globally. Cross-WASM COW sync is a REQUIRED mechanism, and no design may be chosen because it avoids building it.
- **A HOST THAT CANNOT PROVISION A SECOND INSTANCE HAS NOT TESTED THE TRANSPORT, AND EVERY CROSS-INSTANCE MECHANISM ABOVE IS THEN A DESIGN THAT HAS NEVER RUN.** The peer to provision is a CROSS-ORIGIN document — provisioning one for a same-origin child tests a transport that must never carry it. The world registry, the nearest-first ancestry and the lazy segment materialization can all be written, reviewed and self-tested with no peer in existence — and they were, for long enough that grep once answered "a self-test" when asked who calls them. That is the shape a cross-instance design fails in: not a wrong answer, an unexecuted one. The peer it provisions is a second WASM instance, NOT the offscreen — see §Security. The gaps this surfaces are not absence and will not look like it: one member answering from the WRONG AGENT'S RECORD (`closed`, HTML §7.2.2.1 opening and closing windows — a popup that closes itself leaves the opener answering `false` for ever), and a driver aborting on its FIRST reply because a required field was added to the reply record, so every check below that line is unreachable while the file still looks like a passing gate. So a host is not finished when it can ANSWER a cross-document read; it is finished when it PROVISIONS the peer that answers. In a process that means a child PROCESS (the same binary, re-executed with the child's name, address, origin and policy, over a pipe); in the extension it means a second WASM instance the offscreen routes to. What crosses is TEXT and it carries its TYPE, because a live JSValue crosses neither a process, nor an instance, nor a session, nor a park — and because `otherW.length === 0` distinguishes a number from the string "0". A member whose value is an OBJECT does not cross at all until the remote-object handle exists: answering one by serializing it is how a cross-document read starts returning something that is not the thing, so the encoder CRASHES there instead.
- **A PEER ANSWERS BY RUNNING A PROGRAM, never by reading a property from C.** The read it is asked for is an IDL getter, and a C activation has no flow base under it — the same rule that makes every other read a flow makes this one one, and it is also what lets an answer that has to suspend exist at all.
- **A SYNCHRONOUS CROSS-INSTANCE READ IS A SUSPEND POINT — that is what the primitive is for.** The cross-origin allowlist is not all navigable-own state: `otherW.length` is the child-navigable count of the ACTIVE DOCUMENT and `otherW.closed` tracks the peer, so a cross-origin read must answer on the line that made it while the answer lives in another instance. (Same-origin, `iframe.contentWindow.document.body` is answered in-heap in that same turn, because the two documents are one agent — a suspend there would be OBSERVABLE and therefore a fidelity bug, not extra rigor.) At the real boundary the flow SUSPENDS exactly as it suspends at an `await` or a loop back-edge, the peer instance resolves the read in its own scheduled turn under the requesting flow's world, and the flow RESUMES with the value — byte-identical, at any depth. Sibling flows run meanwhile, which is the point. A same-origin `about:blank` child (`window.open()` with no argument, an `<iframe>` with no `src`) is created synchronously by the spec, and its CSP is not a special case: every Document has a §7.1.7 Policy containers POLICY CONTAINER, and §7.3.2.1 Creating browsing contexts CLONES the creator's when there is one — clone it ACROSS the boundary like any other cross-instance state.
- **HTML §7.2.1 security infrastructure for Window, WindowProxy, and Location objects — ITS SAME-ORIGIN CHECK IS DECLARED BESIDE THE MEMBERS, never asked at the read.** A WindowProxy exposes a FIXED cross-origin list (window, self, location, close, closed, focus, blur, frames, length, top, opener, parent, postMessage) and everything else is same-origin only; a read outside it is a SecurityError, thrown at the read, because a page distinguishes that from `undefined`. The failure mode of checking per-read is SILENCE: this surface had no check at all and was safe only by the accident that the members built so far were nearly the whole list — the first one added without one would have leaked a cross-origin document with nothing to say so. An OPAQUE origin is same-origin with NOTHING, not even another opaque one, which is the same rule SECURITY.md states for the credentialed-read principal because it is the same concept. Both halves are the assertion: a filter that threw for everything passes a test that only checks the throw.
- **A cross-document message is an EMISSION, never shared mutation** — immutable once posted, so nothing has to un-send it when the sending flow parks or is outranked, and it needs no COW capture. It carries three things and each is load-bearing: the SERIALIZED bytes (never a live value — a live value crosses neither an instance, nor a session, nor a park); the SENDER'S ORIGIN stamped by the TRUSTED ZONE, never by the untrusted engine (the same rule as keying authorization on `sender.tab.url` — a forgeable `event.origin` defeats every origin check in every bundle, and would make the solver report exploits that are not real and miss ones that are); and the SENDING FLOW'S WORLD, its decision vector. Delivery SEEDS a flow in the receiver whose world is receiver-baseline ∧ that vector: two arms of a fork post two messages belonging to two contradictory worlds, and merging them fabricates a timeline neither sender was in. The offscreen ROUTES, because it is the only zone that knows which instance holds which document. No second scheduler — a delivery is a work item on the ONE frontier.

# Build
`node engine/build.mjs cow` (`extension/lib/qjs/`). Browser/solver work is gated behind test262-with-features support.

# Testing — FEATURE test262 (`node engine/test262.mjs [subdir]`) + the solver differential (`node engine/solvergate.mjs [doc]`)
- **A GATE RUN FROM THE WORKING TREE MEASURES A TREE THAT NO LONGER EXISTS.** The checkout is SHARED and edited continuously, so a build reads its inputs at different instants: `idl_args.h` gained a field 33 seconds before a link finished, `media_query_list.c` compiled `IdlDictMember` at 56 bytes and `idl_args.c` at 64, and the program segfaulted in `strcmp` inside a `DFAIL`'s own order check — a confident false red whose only symptom was a struct read one slot early. That is the loaded-machine defect one layer up: not a wrong number, a number about NOTHING, because no revision of the tree ever contained the program that ran. So a gate runs from a FROZEN SNAPSHOT — `git clone --shared --no-checkout` of the repository plus the same of the submodule, each `git checkout --detach`ed at the revision you mean to measure — and the commit it measured is REPORTED WITH THE RESULT. It is a CLONE and not a worktree for the reason §A-PRIVATE-INDEX-IS-BANNED gives: the snapshot exists to be unreachable from the tree that is moving, and a worktree shares that tree's `.git`. `--shared` means the objects are not copied, so the freeze costs seconds rather than a gigabyte; what a clone will NOT bring is anything git does not track, so a toolchain under an ignored path is provisioned into the snapshot deliberately — SYMLINKED where it is a pure toolchain that no build writes to, and COPIED where the build reads it as an input, so the frozen run cannot write back into the tree every other lane is sharing. **AND THE OBJECT CACHE IS THE INPUT THAT SENTENCE NAMES AND NOBODY APPLIES IT TO, BECAUSE IT READS AS PART OF THE TOOLCHAIN AND IS PART OF THE MEASUREMENT.** The rule above draws the line correctly — SYMLINK a pure toolchain, COPY anything the build writes to — and a compiled-object directory sits on the wrong side of it while looking like the right one: it lives beside `emsdk` under the same ignored path, it is nobody's source, and sharing it is what makes a freeze cost seconds instead of minutes. So it gets symlinked, and the freeze is then defeated in BOTH directions at once by one line: the build READS objects compiled from other revisions, and it WRITES its own back for the next snapshot to read. **THE SNAPSHOT ISOLATES SOURCES AND SHARES THE THINGS THE SOURCES WERE COMPILED INTO, WHICH IS THE ONE INPUT THAT STILL MOVES** — a gate whose whole purpose is a number belonging to a revision, taken against a program assembled from several. Measured: 13498 objects in an 836 MB directory symlinked into every frozen build of a session, discovered only when a lane reported that its commit changed a STRUCT SIZE and warned that the next build had to be clean. That is the read-one-slot-early defect with a cache in place of a race, and this project has already paid for it once — a segfault inside `strcmp` in a `DFAIL`'s own order check, from a program no revision contained. **AND THE HAZARD IS INVISIBLE PRECISELY WHEN IT IS WORST**: a cache is right almost always, so the one commit it is wrong about is the one nobody suspects, and the artifact it produces LINKS and RUNS. So the object directory is **PRIVATE AND EMPTY PER SNAPSHOT** — not a copy of the shared one, since a copy carries exactly the stale objects that cause the defect, and not a hardlink farm, since a build that writes in place would corrupt the original. The price is a full compile per gate run and it is the correct price: a number that belongs to a revision is the entire product of a freeze, and a cache that saves minutes by spending that is not an optimisation. **THE GENERAL TEST, WHICH OUTLIVES THIS DIRECTORY: for every path a snapshot shares rather than copies, ask whether the BUILD WRITES TO IT — and if it does, it is not a toolchain, it is state, and sharing state between two measurements is how one measurement becomes the other's input.** The tell is a provisioning line justified by speed. **AND A SUBMODULE IS THE CASE THAT SENTENCE MISSES, BECAUSE IT IS TRACKED AND STILL ARRIVES EMPTY.** The rule above reasons about what git does not TRACK, and a submodule is tracked — so it reads as covered and is not: a clone records the gitlink and populates NOTHING, leaving the directory with zero entries. A frozen snapshot made exactly as prescribed therefore measures a tree with a hole in it, and the hole is not a small one where the engine's own fork lives. **THE FAILURE IS SILENT BY CONSTRUCTION, BECAUSE THE TOOLS GUARD THE PATH RATHER THAN REQUIRE IT.** An instrument that adds a file with `if (exists(p))` skips it without a word, and prints a resolved-of-total that is a fraction of a population missing its largest member — §a-coverage-figure-states-what-it-is-a-fraction-of, arriving through the freeze procedure instead of through the instrument's own logic. MEASURED: a lane froze a snapshot to audit citations, and reported the fork's own cluster of them as "outside the auditor entirely, before and after". It was outside THAT RUN; the file is in the default set and always was, and the directory was empty. The claim that followed — that the walk excludes the submodule by name — was half true and read as a policy, which is the worst outcome, because a policy is something a reader stops questioning. So the freeze provisions the SUBMODULE beside the toolchain, at the revision the superproject's gitlink names — never at whatever the working checkout happens to hold, which is the stale-gitlink defect one directory down. And the standing check is cheap: **after freezing, count the entries in every submodule path before believing any number the snapshot produces.** An empty directory and a clean result are indistinguishable in a total. **AND THE GATE ABOVE CANNOT FIRE FOR AN AD-HOC HARNESS, BECAUSE `git archive` OMITS THE SUBMODULE PATH ENTIRELY RATHER THAN LEAVING IT EMPTY.** The freeze procedure's cure is to COUNT the entries under every submodule path, and that presupposes a path to count: a clone creates the directory and populates nothing, so the count is zero and the check speaks. `git archive` records a gitlink as no files at all, so there is no directory, nothing to count, and **the prescribed check has nothing to fire on** — the one instrument this file names for exactly this hazard is silent by construction against the commonest way a reader assembles a tree in a hurry. A verification harness built out of `git archive` therefore inherits NONE of the freeze's gates while looking like a freeze. **AND THE FAILURE ARRIVES AS A RED, WHICH IS THE DIRECTION THAT COSTS SOMETHING**: everything else in this file about a missing submodule produces a quiet undercount, and this produces a confident accusation — a compile against a tree with the engine's own fork missing fails loudly, names a real symbol, and reads as a defect in the diff under test. Measured, by a coordinator verifying a peer's commit before publishing it: an out-of-tree syntax check failed on an undeclared function, was reported as a real failure of that commit, and `origin/main` under the identical method failed the same way at the same line — so the harness was at fault and the commit was fine. It was one sentence from a peer's verified work being withheld. **THE DISCRIMINATOR IS THE BASELINE AND IT IS ONE COMMAND, AND IT IS THE COMMAND NOBODY RUNS WHEN THE ANSWER IS RED**: a green result invites no further work, so the reflex to check the baseline fires only on success, which is exactly backwards — a red is a claim about a DIFFERENCE and cannot be read from one side. Run the same harness at the parent BEFORE reporting any failure it produces, and where both sides fail identically, the finding is about your method. The tell that you are about to do this: you assembled a tree with a command you chose for speed, and the symbol it cannot find belongs to a component you did not touch. **AND THE SUBMODULE IS THE CASE WHERE THE BYTES ARE TRACKED AND ARRIVE EMPTY; THE WORSE ONE IS BYTES THAT ARE NOT TRACKED AT ALL, BECAUSE NO COUNT OF ENTRIES CAN EVER FIND THEM.** The rule above has a cure — count the entries — and that cure presupposes the thing being counted is IN the repository. A gate's INPUTS often are not: a served fixture, a golden file, a recorded payload, anything a `.gitignore` line swept up because it sat beside generated output. A frozen snapshot is a CLONE, so it contains exactly what git tracks and nothing else — which means a fixture-driven gate run from a snapshot serves NOTHING, and the freeze this file mandates for reproducibility is the very step that removes the inputs. **THE FAILURE IS SILENT AND IT WEARS THE SUBJECT'S CLOTHES**: a fixture server with no fixture answers 404, and a test that receives a 404 for its own fixture reads as a test whose SUBJECT failed rather than as a harness that was never provisioned — the §A-QUESTION-SOME-ENTRIES-ASK shape, with the wrong component named. Measured, and found by a lane that went to ADD a fixture rather than by any audit: a project's whole served-fixture directory was ignored by one unreasoned line, so **every fixture it had existed in no revision**, while the same ignore file argued the opposite case a few lines below. It is invisible to every instrument here — a collection audit walks what is on DISK and finds them all, an entry count finds a populated directory, and only `git ls-files` over the path answers zero. **SO THE STANDING CHECK IS ONE COMMAND AND IT IS ABOUT THE REPOSITORY RATHER THAN THE DISK: `git ls-tree -r <the revision you froze> -- <the input path>`, and it is owed before any number a fixture produced is quoted at a revision.** The general form outlives fixtures: **for every input a gate reads, ask whether the REVISION contains it, not whether the working tree does** — the two agree for source and routinely disagree for everything else, and the whole value of a frozen measurement is that it belongs to a revision. A gate whose inputs are untracked does not produce a number belonging to no revision; it produces one belonging to whoever's working tree it happened to run in, which is worse, because that is not visible in the result either. **AND THE SNAPSHOT IS CHECKED OUT AT AN EXPLICIT SHA, NEVER AT A REF NAME — BECAUSE A CLONE'S `origin/*` IS BUILT FROM THE SOURCE REPOSITORY'S *LOCAL* BRANCHES, NOT FROM ITS REMOTE-TRACKING ONES.** This is the rule §diff-against-`origin/main` reaches for, arriving inside the very procedure that was supposed to make a number quotable, and inverted: inside a snapshot, `origin/main` means *the parent's local `main`* — which in a shared checkout is whatever a lane committed and has not pushed. So a baseline frozen "at `origin/main`" can be the very commit you are trying to measure against, and the before/after is then **the diff compared with itself**: both sides identical, every column unchanged, and nothing anywhere saying the comparison was empty. MEASURED: a lane committed locally, froze a baseline at `origin/main` inside the clone, and got byte-identical numbers on both sides — it was one sentence from reporting an auditor as already complete on a member that did not exist. `<sha>^` gave the real baseline. Demonstrated directly afterwards: a fresh `--shared` clone's `origin/<branch>` resolved to a **stale local branch head** while the true remote stood elsewhere. **THE TELL IS A BEFORE/AFTER WHOSE TWO SIDES AGREE EXACTLY**, which reads as "my change was inert" and is far more often "I measured one revision twice". Resolve every revision in the PARENT with `git rev-parse` BEFORE cloning, pass the SHA to `checkout --detach`, and print both SHAs beside the result — a snapshot that cannot name what it is is a snapshot whose number belongs to nothing. **AND THE SNAPSHOT MUST BE AT A PATH NOBODY ELSE WILL CHOOSE, BECAUSE THE SCRATCH DIRECTORY IS SHARED STATE TOO AND A COLLIDING NAME UNFREEZES IT.** The whole point of the snapshot is that its bytes cannot move under the measurement; a second agent that picks the same obvious name — `frozen/`, `base/`, `before/` — reaches in and moves them, and the run that was carefully isolated from the working tree is measuring a directory two agents are writing. Measured: one lane's frozen clone was clobbered mid-run by a peer and it caught the collision only because its numbers stopped reproducing; it re-froze under a private path and re-measured. The failure is quiet in the usual way — a scan completes and prints — and it is the same defect as running from the working tree, arriving through the place chosen to escape it. So name a snapshot after the LANE and the REVISION, not after its role. A result quoted without the revision it came from is not a measurement, and "I rebuilt and it went away" is the tell that the first build measured the edit rather than the tree. **AND THE SNAPSHOT THAT MAKES A NUMBER QUOTABLE IS EXACTLY WHAT MAKES ITS DIAGNOSTICS STALE, WHICH IS THE TRAP ON THE OTHER SIDE OF THIS RULE.** A frozen build is pinned so its RESULT belongs to a revision — and every warning, assert and abort it printed is pinned with it. Those read as facts about the tree, and they are facts about a revision that a shared checkout has already left behind: the log is fresh, the reasoning is sound, and the defect it names was fixed hours ago. It is the stale-`DFAIL` failure mode arriving through the instrument this file PRESCRIBES, so the discipline that protects the measurement is what corrupts the relay. Measured: a compiler warning (`incompatible pointer types passing 'long long *' to parameter of type 'int64_t *'`) was read out of a frozen build's log and relayed to a lane as live work; the receiving lane found the file already carried `int64_t` at `origin/main`, with a comment recording the fix, and the only thing at that line in the working tree was a THIRD lane's unrelated hunk — so the relay would have sent someone to re-fix a fixed line inside a file somebody else was holding. **So a diagnostic is quoted with the revision that produced it, exactly like a number, and is RE-READ AT `origin/main` before it is relayed as work** — the number is what the frozen revision is for, and the diagnostic is what it is not. The tell is that you are about to describe a defect in the present tense from a log you did not just produce. The corollary is the reason this is not a burden: an agent that cannot build is not being denied a gate, it is being kept out of the shared tree's way, and the one process that DOES build owes every agent the revision its number belongs to. **AND IT IS NOT ONLY BUILDS — A STATIC SCAN READS THE SAME MOVING TREE, AND ITS FAILURE IS THE QUIETER ONE.** Everything above is about a build reading its inputs at different instants, and a build at least CRASHES when it does — a struct read one slot early segfaults. A scan does not crash: it completes and prints a number. Measured with several lanes live: three consecutive runs of ONE scan found the corpus digest changed under EVERY ONE of them, four distinct digests across three attempts, so no stable window existed at all; and an earlier run of that same scan reported 98 broken contracts because a peer's half-written file was read mid-scan, which INVERTS the signal exactly, since every field of one census loses its reader at once. Neither number was noisy — both were about NOTHING, and nothing in the output said so. So the frozen snapshot is the rule for EVERY instrument that reads the tree, `citegen`/`fieldgate`/`idlgen` included, and a BEFORE/AFTER is two runs on ONE frozen state — a baseline copy of the changed script with the change reverted, every other input shared, both runs back to back — never one run before an edit and one after, because the tree moved between them and the difference you attribute to your diff is mostly somebody else's. **AND THAT PRESCRIPTION HAS A HOLE THIS FILE PUT THERE, FOUND BY A LANE OBEYING IT: `EVERY OTHER INPUT SHARED` IS FALSE FOR AN INSTRUMENT THAT INFERS FROM ITS CORPUS, BECAUSE THE BASELINE COPY'S LOCATION IS ITSELF AN INPUT.** The method above assumes a tool's answer about a file depends on that file. Many auditors here do not work that way: they PLACE an ambiguous datum by voting over its neighbours — a file vote, a dominant anchor, the surrounding citations — so the same bytes at a scratch path sit among different neighbours and are placed differently. The baseline is then not the same program measured twice; it is a DIFFERENT CORPUS, and the delta you attribute to your diff includes every re-placement the move caused. **THE TELL IS THAT THE MOVEMENT APPEARS WHERE YOUR DIFF DOES NOT REACH**: measured by a lane that declined to quote its own numbers, a whole-run pair over baseline copies moved a VERIFIED population by a third on a handful of added quotations, and flipped two accusation categories in areas the diff did not touch — a change of that shape is the instrument re-deciding, not the subject changing. **THE CONFOUND-FREE MEASUREMENT IS AN INTERSECTION RATHER THAN A DIFFERENCE**: run the tool ONCE, over the real tree, and keep only the findings whose LINE NUMBERS fall inside the lines your diff added. That answers *what did I introduce* — the question actually being asked — without ever constructing a second corpus, and it is immune to peers editing between runs, which is the other half of what makes whole-run pairs unquotable in a shared checkout. So the general rule: **before taking a before/after, ask whether the instrument's answer about a datum can depend on data OTHER than that datum. Where it can, a copy is not a control, and the honest measurement is the one that never moves the bytes. **AND THE SAME QUESTION IS OWED ABOUT MOVING THE INSTRUMENT, WHERE THE FAILURE IS SILENCE RATHER THAN A SHIFTED NUMBER.** The rule above is about copying the DATA to get a baseline; the other half of a before/after is copying the TOOL — checking out its previous version to a scratch path and running that. An instrument that locates its own corpus, index or config RELATIVE TO ITSELF then finds none of it, and what it prints is not a wrong figure but NOTHING — which renders exactly like a clean run and invites you to write the blank down as the baseline. Measured, by me, on this project's citation auditor: a `BEFORE` line came back empty and the only reason it was not reported as data is that the `AFTER` line beside it was obviously well-formed. That is §THE-VERIFICATION-CAN-FAIL-IN-THE-VERIFIER arriving through the tool's own path resolution, and the tell is the same one — an empty answer at a revision where you KNOW the subject exists. **SO A TOOL IS COMPARED AGAINST ITSELF IN PLACE, NEVER FROM A COPY**, which for a shared tree means the honest form is a pair of runs back-to-back on ONE state with the instrument's own DIGEST printed at both ends. Two lanes arrived at that discipline independently on the same day, for a reason worth stating: an instrument another lane is editing moves under you, and its output moves for files you never touched — so a run before your edit and a run after it differ by their work as much as by yours, and only a same-instrument pair separates them. Print the digest, or the numbers belong to no revision and no tool..** And a lane that reports a whole-run pair as CONFOUNDED and refuses to quote it has done the harder and more valuable thing than one that quotes a clean-looking delta. A scan run against the live tree anyway yields a number belonging to NO REVISION, and saying so is part of reporting it.
- **A SUBAGENT DOES NOT BUILD. THE MAIN AGENT OWNS EVERY BUILD, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE OF THE DIVISION.** No `build.mjs`, no compiler, no gate `.mjs`. A subagent reads, greps, CURLS, reasons, writes the change, and DRIVES THE ALREADY-BUILT ARTIFACT — `testing/harness.js` against the wasm the main agent last built costs no compile, so reproducing a real page's abort in real Chrome is a subagent's job and always was. What it must not do is compile.
  **THE REASON IS ARITHMETIC AND IT WAS MEASURED HERE.** A build is HUNDREDS of translation units against a handful of cores, so N agents building is not N times the work — it is one queue with N claimants. A session that let ten agents build reached a 26-DEEP QUEUE at load 8.4, with one agent waiting 69 MINUTES and NO COMPILER RUNNING, because a build holds its slot through a 15-minute smoke stage that is a known pre-existing timeout and tells nobody anything. Nine parallel agents produced less than one serial agent would. **AND THE MAGNITUDE THAT USED TO CARRY THIS ARGUMENT WAS ONE FIGURE DOING DUTY FOR TWO PROGRAMS, WHICH IS WHY IT IS GONE.** A single translation-unit count stood in both this sentence and the one about the corpus runner — two DIFFERENT programs with different source sets — and a third, larger figure sat a few lines away as a measured incident, so the file disagreed with itself about its own build three times over. Re-derived: the private object directory a single frozen build fills is far larger than any of them, because the count anyone quotes is one target's sources and the build assembles several. That is §a-bare-count-over-a-population-you-have-not-partitioned arriving in the rule that decides who may build, and the rule never needed the number: **the argument is the RATIO — many units, few cores, one queue with N claimants — and the evidence is the measured consequence beneath it**, which is a fact about an incident rather than a property of a tree that grows every week. A magnitude in a justification rots and takes the rule down with it, because a reader who finds the number wrong discards the sentence it was propping up. So where a count is load-bearing, hand over the DERIVATION as a command and say which target it counts — `ls <the build's own object directory> | wc -l` after a build, named — and where it is merely rhetorical, drop it and keep the mechanism. A LOCK DOES NOT FIX THIS — it converts the thrash into a queue and the queue is still hours deep; the only thing that fixes it is one builder.
  `curl` is REQUIRED: a subagent has no WebFetch, so curling the spec is the only way it can meet §Browser half's obligation to verify a cited section number and TITLE against the text rather than recall it. A number nobody fetched is a number nobody may write.
  What a subagent produces is the reasoned change with its asserts, plus a statement of WHAT NEEDS BUILDING and AT WHICH REVISION; the main agent builds it and returns the number with its revision pair. VERIFICATION BY RUNNING WAS NEVER THE GATE HERE — §Offensive programming: the engine asserts its own invariants at their origin, so a change is verified by whether the asserts stay silent, and a passing run proves nothing it did not exercise. A subagent blocked on a build is usually one that should be reading the ownership contract at the site that breaks it. **AND THE WAY THIS RULE GETS BROKEN IS NOT DISOBEDIENCE — IT IS THAT A SCRIPT WHOSE TOP LEVEL IS THE WORK CANNOT BE CHECKED BY IMPORTING IT.** `node --check` parses and does not resolve identifiers, so a name used before it is defined passes — which has already left one instrument throwing for every lane in this shared tree. The obvious escalation is to import the module and see whether it loads. For `build.mjs` that IS the build: an agent doing exactly that compiled 302 translation units and **overwrote `engine/host/out/qjs.{js,wasm}` and `extension/lib/qjs/qjs.mjs` in the SHARED tree**, from a working copy holding its own uncommitted files plus a peer's in-progress ones — an artifact belonging to no revision, sitting where every harness driver looks. That is §A-GATE-RUN-FROM-THE-WORKING-TREE's defect PRODUCED rather than suffered, and it is silent: the file is newer, not broken, so the next `testing/harness.js` drive measures a program no commit contains and reports a number for it. So a `.mjs` that does work at import is exercised on a COPY or not at all, its entry point is asked before it is loaded, and an agent that runs one says so in its report — which this one did, prominently, which is the only reason it is written down here instead of discovered next week.
- **A BULK GATE IS RUN ONCE, BY THE MAIN AGENT, AND SUBAGENTS REQUEST IT — they never run it themselves.** test262, WPT and solvergate are whole-corpus runs over a SHARED tree on a SHARED machine, so N subagents each running one is not N times the coverage, it is the SAME number computed N times at N different unknown revisions, and it is worse than wasteful: concurrent corpus runs are the loaded-machine defect manufacturing itself. The four worked examples above are all one machine under load, and the arithmetic is not marginal — a 4-core box at load 7.13 makes an elapsed-time kill (test262 at ~590 s, WPT at 60 s per test) fire on healthy code, so every agent's run corrupts every other agent's number AND its own. Two agents waiting on the same corpus is not parallelism at all; it is one gate run twice, serialized by the CPU rather than by anybody's decision, with neither copy trustworthy. So: **the main agent owns the gates and serializes them.** A subagent that needs one states WHAT IT NEEDS TO KNOW and at WHICH revision, and gets back the result with its revision pair — one run answers every agent that asked. What a subagent still does for itself is the TARGETED exercise: one fixture, one file, `test_forced.c`, or a single document driven through an ALREADY-BUILT artifact — **and `wpt.mjs` is NOT that, which this file said for as long as the sentence stood.** Its runner compiles the WHOLE PROGRAM of clang UNCONDITIONALLY before it executes anything, so `a single wpt.mjs path` was an instruction to do the one thing this rule forbids, and it was dispatched repeatedly before a lane read the file and refused it. **THE REFUSAL WAS THE DELIVERABLE**: the lane declined a run its brief explicitly authorised, said so, and downgraded its own oracle claim from a measurement to a prediction rather than quoting a build it should not have made. The general shape is worth more than the instance — **a driver's NAME says what it drives and never what it BUILDS FIRST**, so before authorising any tool as cheap, grep it for a compiler invocation; and where a brief and the tree disagree about what a command costs, the tree is the one that was measured — that is not the bulk gate, it is how a change is exercised so its asserts get a chance to fire, and it is cheap enough to be nobody's bottleneck. **The DCHECKs remain the gate** (§the engine asserts its own invariants at their origin); the corpus is how code gets EXERCISED, never the thing that certifies a change, so a subagent blocked on a corpus number is usually a subagent that should be reading the ownership contract at the site instead.
- **THE SOLVER'S SEMANTICS HAVE THEIR OWN ORACLE, AND IT IS THE ONE THING THE FINDINGS MUST NOT DEPEND ON.** test262 links FIVE quickjs sources and not one file under `engine/host`, so a green corpus says nothing whatever about `decide.c`, `cow.c`, `concolic.c`, `flow.c`, `engine.c` or `cold.c`; WPT links them but judges them by a browser oracle, catching a solver bug only when it also breaks a spec answer. The missing gate is a DIFFERENTIAL: for a document whose frontier drains, the finding set is a function of the DOCUMENT ALONE, so the same document under several schedules (default, yield-on-every-step, replies answered tail-first, parked and resumed in a second instance) must emit the SAME findings. It stores no expected output, so a solver that learns more passes unchanged and there is nothing to update when the solver improves — it fails only when one build disagrees with ITSELF, which is exactly the §scheduler razor (drops, starves, skips, reorders, forgets ⇒ a cap) made measurable. A per-document expected-emission list is the banned shape twice over: a change detector, and a second hand-maintained copy of `test_forced.c`.
- **The project is NEVER run without its features, so a plain conformance pass tests something that never runs.** There is NO non-feature mode and NO fallback to plain `JS_Eval` (for modules, async, anything) — a fallback hides feature gaps and is banned. test262 is SELF-VALIDATING (each file carries its own oracle: `assert.*` + `negative:` metadata), so a `run-test262` error already means the FEATURE engine got the spec answer wrong — running upstream to "confirm" is pointless (upstream is only the `git bisect` floor). ONE run: every stock file driven through the flow machinery under FORCED time-travel (preempt every loop back-edge ⇒ thousands of heap-frame suspend/rebuild cycles; async/generators through the driver seam). An unsupported construct FAILS LOUD (an honest gap to build at the root, e.g. module support in `JS_FlowNew`), never papered over.
- **A passing RESULT does not prove the feature ENGAGED — the harness measures both.** `JS_FlowPreemptStats` reports preempt-requested vs fired; their divergence is exactly where the feature was gated (a body with no adopting driver), so the harness FAILS any run below 100% engagement — a category can never masquerade as green while the feature silently skipped its logic, regardless of codebase state. Correctness comes from the test262 oracle; coverage comes from engagement. **BUT ENGAGEMENT IS A RATIO, AND A RATIO CANNOT SEE WORK THAT NEVER STARTED — SO 100% IS A STATEMENT ABOUT ITS DENOMINATOR AND NOT ABOUT THE CORPUS.** Engagement divides fired preempts by requested ones, and both are counted over code that EXECUTED; a body no run ever entered contributes to neither side, so it cannot lower the figure and its absence is unrepresented in it. That is a different hole from the one engagement was built to catch — that one is a body that ran with the feature gated OFF, and this one is a body that never ran — and the metric is blind to the second by construction. Measured: `test262.conf` carried `mode=default`, which the corpus's own INTERPRETING.md contradicts in a MUST ("each test must be executed twice: once in ECMAScript's non-strict mode, and again in ECMAScript's strict mode"), so **39620 of 43523 files bore none of the four exempting flags and were executed sloppy ONLY — 47.7% of required executions never performed**. The run reported `0/43520 errors, 0 leaks, feature: 100.0% engaged` and every one of those numbers was true of the half that ran. It is also worse than an excluded file, which at least appears in a NOT-RUN count: the file WAS collected, so the missing execution appears in no exclusion list, no skip tally and no NOT-RUN line at all. **So a coverage figure states WHAT IT IS A FRACTION OF, in the same line, or it is not a coverage figure** — and a count of files says nothing about executions when a file owes more than one. Run the WHOLE corpus (default, no arg) for the honest number — cherry-picking a clean subdir and calling a category "supported" is banned self-deception. Leaks are surfaced by the `gc_obj_list` walk in `JS_FreeRuntime` (built NDEBUG so a leak logs+counts as a failure). Test all categories; the time-travel features must be transformed into every one, in spec order so no subproblem is skipped. Use design review, not a test metric as a target (a smaller number is not automatically worse). Browser tests are in C. Delete a regression test after use — it prevents better designs.
- **A TEST FILE THE GATE DOES NOT COLLECT IS AN EXCLUDED TEST, AND AN EXCLUDED TEST IS A FAILURE.** **AND ONE LEVEL BELOW THAT IS A TEST THAT IS NOT ON DISK, WHICH NO COLLECTION AUDIT CAN SEE EITHER.** A collection rule assumes the corpus is the corpus. This one is a SPARSE checkout (`core.sparseCheckout = true`), so whole areas are absent from the working tree entirely — and a gate that walks what it finds, collects correctly, runs everything it collected and reports honest pass counts is SILENT about every area outside the cone. That is worse than an uncollected file, which at least exists to be found by an audit of the collector. Measured: a lane repairing a defect that **every page loading jQuery takes** searched for its own member across the whole corpus and found it exactly ONCE, in an IDL file — `html/semantics/forms` is not in the cone, so NO gate in this tree judges any of it, which is why the defect survived. **THE CHECK IS ONE COMMAND AND IT IS OWED BEFORE ANY CLAIM THAT A CORPUS COVERS SOMETHING**: read the sparse cone, not the directory listing, and quote WHICH AREAS ARE IN IT beside any coverage number. A pass count over an unknown fraction of a corpus is the §a-coverage-figure-states-what-it-is-a-fraction-of defect with the denominator not merely unstated but unfetched. It is the same defect as a directory that is not checked out, and worse, because the total LOOKS complete: a gate that collected `.any.js` and `.window.js` only left more than half the checked-out corpus — nearly all of `html/browsers` — never run and never counted. WPT classifies a test document by what it LOADS (a file that pulls in `/resources/testharness.js`), never by a naming convention, and so does this gate. An HTML test is FETCHED from the corpus's own server rather than read off disk: a `.sub.html` is a TEMPLATE whose hosts and ports wptserve substitutes, and running the bytes on disk tests the template. **AND A TEST THAT IS COLLECTED, RUNS, AND REPORTS IS STILL NOT A TEST THAT MEASURED ANYTHING — ITS OWN FIXTURES CAN BE UNREACHABLE, AND THEN IT RETURNS A SMALL HONEST NUMBER THAT READS AS A SMALL HONEST RESULT.** The excluded-test rule above is about files the gate never collects, and a collection audit can find those. This is the case AFTER collection is correct, which is why no audit here could see it: every `idlharness` document in the corpus was reached, ran, and reported — **exactly two subtests each**, for its setup and its harness, because the harness fetches its own `.idl` from a directory the collector did not serve. THAT PARTICULAR GAP IS CLOSED, AND THIS SENTENCE OUTLIVED IT: a lane sent to fix it found the directory checked out and answering 200 over the wire, so the coordinates here had become a claim about a tree that had moved while the DEFECT SHAPE stayed true. Do not read the fixture path as live work; re-derive it. The family passed every self-check the gate makes about its own collection and **had never executed one IDL assertion in this tree**, while being the only oracle for a property no local instrument can ask. The diagnostic existed exactly ONCE: one document said `Error fetching /interfaces/cssom.idl` in words and the other twenty-two timed out awaiting the same promise, so twenty-two copies of one cause wore the shape of a slow test. So a gate reports a test's SUBTEST COUNT beside its verdict, and a family whose members all report a floor of two is a family to open — and when a test declares fixtures (a fetched IDL, a serviced resource, a substituted host), the reachability of those fixtures is part of collecting it, not a separate question the test is left to discover at runtime. The gate reports PER AREA as well as in total — one number in which `encoding` answers three quarters of a million subtests is a number in which every other component is invisible.
- **A MEASUREMENT THAT A LOADED MACHINE CAN FALSIFY IS NOT A MEASUREMENT, AND A WALL CLOCK IS THE USUAL WAY IN.** Four separate checks in one session reported an artifact of HOW they ran as a defect in WHAT ran, and each one produced a confident false red that sent someone hunting a phantom: the seam assertion fired at 14486 ms of ELAPSED time with `points asked=0` and could not tell a stretch that offered no suspend point from one that was DESCHEDULED (a descheduled thread executes no instructions and so reaches no preempt check either, which is why that second number did not save it); `g_tramp_frames_live` was one process-wide count for run-test262's SEVERAL corpus threads, so its unwound-chain DCHECK fired on a race between two healthy threads; `wpt.mjs` killed a test after 60 s of elapsed time and reported it in the same column as a DCHECK naming a missing capability, so `dom/ranges` read 15743 pass against a 25056 baseline with an IDENTICAL fail count; and `test262.mjs` killed the corpus at 590 s and reported it through a message naming three causes it distinguished none of ("segfault/abort/timeout"), so a healthy binary read as a corpus-wide crash. **MEASURE THE THING THE INVARIANT IS ABOUT** — CPU actually consumed (`RLIMIT_CPU`), or work actually performed (forks + flows + jobs), never elapsed time; where the host has no CPU clock, CHECK that rather than assume it (emscripten answers every WASI clock from `emscripten_get_now()`, and musl's `clock()` lands in the same place). **A budget that must remain for a case the real measure cannot see — a deadlock consuming no CPU — is a BACKSTOP, is generous, and reports through a DIFFERENT SIGNAL so the two never collapse into one verdict** (`SIGXCPU` from the kernel, `SIGTERM` from the harness). Print both numbers, name which one is the verdict, and label the artifact case with the load average that explains it. The tell that you are looking at this defect rather than a real one: **a kill leaves an EMPTY tail where a crash leaves the output that preceded it**. **AND THE BUILD MANUFACTURES THE LOAD THAT BIASES ITS OWN LATER STAGES, WHICH IS THIS DEFECT ARRIVING FROM INSIDE THE INSTRUMENT'S OWN SCHEDULE.** Everything above is about a measurement corrupted by OTHER work on the box. This is the same corruption with no other work in sight: a build compiles hundreds of translation units and then runs its gates, so every stage after the compile measures a machine the compile just loaded — and on a host whose quantum is wall-denominated, load decides how far a flow replays before a peer answers. The stage does not know it is being measured under a transient, and the transient is caused by the very process running it. **MEASURED, and it inverts a verdict rather than blurring a number**: a routing gate reported PASS in-build and FAILED 12 of 12 standalone at the SAME revision with the SAME command; injecting six busy loops on four cores flipped it back to PASS 2 of 3. The gate is bimodal — one mode replays far and fails, the other stops short and passes — so what the build's PASS recorded was its own compile's after-effect, and it had been read as a fact about the revision on every build. The direction is the dangerous one: load makes the run do LESS, and doing less is what passes, so an instrument like this reports GREEN precisely when the machine is least able to exercise it. **SO A GATE'S VERDICT IS A CLAIM ABOUT A REVISION ONLY IF IT REPRODUCES OFF THE BUILD**, run alone on a quiet box, several times, with the load quoted beside it — and a stage that has only ever run inside a build has a verdict nobody has separated from the build's own footprint. The tell is cheap and nobody was checking it: a gate that has never been run standalone. **AND THE LOAD IS NOT ONLY FROM WORK THAT IS RUNNING — IT IS FROM WORK THAT FINISHED AND DID NOT CLEAN UP, WHICH THE OBVIOUS CHECK CANNOT SEE.** "Is anyone else running?" is a question about AGENTS, and a box is loaded by PROCESSES: an agent that reports and exits leaves its browser, its server and its children behind, and those go on competing with every measurement taken afterwards. The two answers diverge without limit, and the divergence grows precisely as a session gets more productive — every completed lane adds to it. MEASURED, on a 4-core box during a live measurement: the agent list showed **three** lanes running, and `ps` showed **565** processes at load 4.49 with 6.0 GB resident. Two browser roots were 2 and 3.2 hours old, and their profile directories NAMED the finished work they belonged to — one of them an artifact revision two builds superseded. Killing those two roots by PID freed **1.85 GB** and dropped the process count to 297, while the live lane's browser survived untouched. Nothing was running that anybody had asked for. **THE PROFILE PATH IS THE EVIDENCE AND IS WHY THIS IS SAFE TO ACT ON**: a process whose working directory or profile names a revision, a lane or an artifact that is no longer current cannot belong to a lane that is current, and a running agent's own instrument is identifiable the same way. Address it by PID — never by pattern, for the reason §A-PROCESS-PATTERN-MATCHES-THE-SHELL-THAT-NAMES-IT gives — and confirm the live one survived rather than assuming it. So before quoting any measurement taken on a shared box, ask what is running that nothing is waiting for; and when a lane finishes, its instruments are part of what it finished with. And the response is never to raise the cap — making a saturated machine report green chooses the number over the forcing function — nor to accept a bounded probe as the corpus result, which is the cherry-picked-subdir self-deception under a new name.
- **ONE RUN OF A LIVE SITE IS NOT A MEASUREMENT, AND A BEFORE/AFTER BUILT FROM TWO OF THEM IS AN ARTIFACT OF THE SITE.** A live page changes under you — its bytes, its server's answers, the order its orphans are reached — so the same artifact against the same URL legitimately returns different numbers, and a single run reads as a clean measurement while carrying all of that variance. One site went 37 endpoints, then 0 with an abort, then 0 without one, across three runs of two builds, and the endpoint column was quoted as a regression before anyone had established the run-to-run spread. **A before/after belongs on FROZEN BYTES — a mirror, a fixture, a recorded payload replayed — where the only thing that changed is the engine.** Live sites are for DISCOVERING signatures, which is what they are uniquely good at: an abort with a real frame list names an unbuilt capability no fixture would have thought to ask for. Report a live number with its run count and its spread, or do not report it as a comparison. **AND THE RULE IS NOT ABOUT LIVE SITES — IT IS ABOUT REPEATABILITY, WHICH IS A PROPERTY YOU MEASURE AND NEVER ONE YOU INFER FROM BOUNDEDNESS.** A local driver that is bounded, offline, single-machine and finishes in seconds reads as deterministic, and that reading is an ASSERTION about a program nobody re-ran. Measured, and it cost a whole bisect: a two-instance driver reported 31 routed posts at one revision and 5 at another, the ratio was quoted as a 6× collapse localised to one commit, and a later lane ran the UNCHANGED pre-fix driver against ONE binary four times and got 5, 5, 31, 31 — then three more on a second build for 5, 31, 5. Seven runs, two modes, no code between them. The shape also PREDATED the accused commit by seventeen hours in a log already on disk. The mechanism was in the instrument's own banner all along (`"measure":"wall … isCpu":false,"sliceMs":12`): a wall-denominated quantum lets machine load pick which side of a race the run lands on. **So the run-count-and-spread obligation attaches to every comparison, and the cheap instruments are where it gets skipped** — precisely because their cheapness is what makes re-running them free. Run it until the spread is known BEFORE attributing a difference to a diff, and never call an instrument deterministic in a report unless a repeat is what established it. **AND ON A SITE THAT ABORTS, THE EMITTED-OUTPUT COLUMN IS NOT A MEASUREMENT OF ANYTHING** — how much a run learned before it died is a function of where the crash landed, so a crashing site's endpoint count moves on its own; the crash record is the stable fact and the count beside it is noise until the crash is gone. **AND THAT MOVEMENT IS NOT ALWAYS NOISE — FIXING A DEFECT AT ITS ORIGIN MOVES THE ABORT UPSTREAM, WHICH DRIVES EVERY REACH COLUMN DOWN, SO A CORRECT ROOT FIX PRESENTS AS A LARGE REGRESSION IN EVERY TOTAL.** The sentence above is about a count that wanders; this is the component of it that has a DIRECTION, and the direction is the one that gets a good diff reverted. §Offensive-programming requires an invariant to be asserted at its ORIGIN rather than where the damage surfaces, so a diff that obeys it necessarily replaces a late abort with an EARLIER one — and everything a run does between the two points stops happening. Measured, across one repair: at the revision before it the smoke aborted downstream having reached 178 frontier members over 3 censuses; at the revision that moved the check to the writer it aborted at 1 member, 1 census, with the fixture answering 3 statements instead of 24. Every total fell by more than an order of magnitude BECAUSE the fix was right. **SO REACH IS NOT COMPARABLE ACROSS A DIFF THAT MOVES AN ASSERT, IN EITHER DIRECTION** — a rise is equally suspect, since deleting a check raises reach for free — and the only honest comparison is between revisions whose abort is the SAME abort. What IS comparable across such a diff is the set of assert IDENTITIES: the old one absent, the new one present, each a fact about one run and neither varying with the interleaving. That is also what makes the pair worth stating together: "6084 gone, 433 fires" is a result, while "reach fell from 24 to 3" is the same result read through a column that cannot express it. **AND A PREDICTION SCORED AGAINST THAT COLUMN MUST BE MARKED UNSCORED RATHER THAN REFUTED** — a discriminator that needs a populated frontier has nothing to be true or false about in a run that aborts before one exists, and recording it as a failure retires a good hypothesis on evidence that never addressed it.
- **A FIXTURE BUILT TO EXERCISE EVERY MECHANISM HAS A WORKLOAD THAT IS A PROPERTY OF ITS DESIGN, SO A READING WHOSE SUBJECT IS THE WORKLOAD'S SHAPE IS PARTLY A STATEMENT ABOUT THE FIXTURE AND NEVER WHOLLY ABOUT THE ENGINE.** The rule above is about ONE RUN against MANY; this is about ONE DOCUMENT against the documents the product actually meets, and it is a different axis that the same instrument serves. A fixture earns its place by asking every question at once — that is what makes it a gate — and asking every question at once means FORKING MAXIMALLY, so its fork factor, its frontier growth, and the proportion of that frontier a single thread gets through are all things its author CHOSE. A real page chose none of them. **THE TRAP IS THAT CHEAPNESS AND AUTHORITY ARRIVE TOGETHER.** The fixture is the one instrument that runs on every build, so its census is the census everybody reads; and it prints inside the build's own verdict block, beside PASS and FAIL, which lends a number about a synthetic workload the standing of a gate result. Nobody has a second census to compare it against, which is exactly the condition under which a bias goes unexamined — not because it is hidden, but because there is nothing beside it. **THE DISCRIMINATOR IS WHETHER THE FIXTURE'S DESIGN SET THE DENOMINATOR.** A ratio of two lifetime counters the fixture did not choose the size of travels; a COUNT, or a proportion of a population whose size is the fixture's own fork factor, does not. "Ninety-two percent of the frontier was never served" is not a fact about a scheduler when the frontier's size is a fixture's design decision — and the same sentence about a real document is. Measured, and stopped one sentence short of the error: a starvation fraction was read climbing monotonically across a run's censuses, the counter's own contract named that trend as the defect reading, and the population it was a fraction of was a frontier the fixture had forked into being. The reading may well hold on a real page and the mechanism behind it may be exactly right — what could not be done was to CALL it a property of the engine from that document, and the cost of finding out was one request to a lane that was already loading real sites. **SO A FIXTURE READING IS A HYPOTHESIS ABOUT REAL DOCUMENTS AND IS REPORTED AS ONE**, with the real-document check named rather than assumed; and where a mechanism is diagnosed from a fixture and repaired, the repair is scored on a document nobody designed. The tell that you are about to transfer one: you are quoting a proportion, and the thing it is a proportion OF is a number the fixture produced rather than a number the world handed you.
- **A DIAGNOSTIC GATED ON A FINDING IS UNREACHABLE ON EXACTLY THE RUNS THAT NEED EXPLAINING, AND THE GATE READS AS AN OPTIMISATION.** The rule is one line: **why a run produced nothing is a different question from what it produced, so it may never be gated on the answer to the second.** Shipping a diagnostic only when there is a finding to attach it to looks like avoiding noise — a run with nothing to report has nothing to say, so why carry the payload. The inversion is total: a run that emits findings is a run you can already reason about, and a run that emits NOTHING is the one whose reasons you need, and that is precisely the run the gate silences. MEASURED: a page's error surface was gated at BOTH ends of an incremental relay on `fetchCallSites.length || securitySinks.length`, which is `0/0` for exactly the pages that learn nothing — and the merge past the gate never carried the error field at all, so the two halves made each other unfalsifiable in the way §A-FIELD-A-CONSUMER-DEFAULTS names. The engine had been recording **hundreds of reported exceptions per run** on a real site, for as long as anyone had been asking why that site emitted nothing, and not one of them could be read. Ungated, they collapsed to a handful of distinct messages and the headline one named an absent platform member at the page's own line and column. **THE COST IS PAID BY THE FORCING FUNCTION.** §NO STUBS makes a missing member an honest ABSENCE whose forcing function is the page's own throw — and a throw nobody can read is not a forcing function, it is a silence. So a gate like this does not merely hide information; it disables the mechanism the design relies on to find work. The tell is greppable and it is a CONJUNCTION: a diagnostic payload whose emission condition mentions a RESULT count. Any predicate of the form "send the errors if there were findings" is this defect, however it is spelled. And the general form to carry into any report: **the run that explains nothing is the run to instrument**, so when you find yourself asking why a subject produced no output, ask FIRST whether the machinery that would tell you is conditioned on output.
- **A RUN THAT SPENDS ITS WHOLE BUDGET WITH NO ABORT IS NOT THEREBY A HEALTHY RUN, AND IT IS ROUTINELY WORSE THAN ONE THAT CRASHES IN TWO SECONDS.** §Offensive-programming says every crash in a dev build is correct and names an unbuilt capability; the corollary nobody states is the one that governs how a REPORT is read — **a terminal event is INFORMATION and a clean budget-exhaustion is not.** An abort names a capability, at a site, with a frame list, and the next diff starts from it. A full budget with no abort names nothing at all, and it is equally consistent with the engine working and with the engine never having reached anything that COULD fail. This is the mirror of the rule that a correct root fix moves an abort UPSTREAM and drives every reach column down: the same asymmetry, read from the other end, where a run gets quieter because it stopped arriving anywhere rather than because it stopped being wrong.
